| harness, RN Web, choreography, the app's own bugs | not Snapback's |

### 10.2 Expectation, stated before the rerun

For Astra at medium: fewer loops (the five language-tax loops, the
pairing loop and the four seam loops of §2.2 are the target), schemas
without work tables or world-readable counters, and a modest wall-clock
gain concentrated in M3 and M8; not a large one, since 0.2.6's
first-green clocks already sit at or under Convex's on five milestones.
For Grok and Muse: the difference between a capability failure and a
green M3, because the two restatements of §3.1 R and the seams of §8 are
where they stopped. Scale (O5) is helped only by legibility (§6.7, §6.8);
horizons, fan-out on write and the feed recipe remain the answer.

### 10.3 The rerun and the ceremony bar

A fresh docs-only cell per builder family on a published pin carrying §5
to §8, on the unchanged frozen instrument; the loop bar (LLP 3000 §3) as
the gate; the 0.2.6 record as the anchor. The rerun decides §9: if
reporting and integrations still cost loops, §9.1 and §9.2 get their
Moves lines; if not, they stay specified and unbuilt.

The loop bar measures M1–M3, where this document expects little to
change. A second bar measures what it claims: **the ceremony bar**, a
`loopbar/` brief on the fixture asking a fresh agent for Insights (six
totals and seven day buckets), Trending (the trailing day by likes),
the digest (once per period, restart-safe), a group's receipt aggregate
and search grouped by chat — the five shapes every lane paid for —
scored by Snapback-attributed loops and wall, N ≥ 3, before and after
the landing. The bar exists so the seven forms, the seeks and `group`
are judged by a number rather than by this document.

**2026-09-15 — the published 0.2.10 full-cell finding.** The
[completed record](../benchmarks/runs/2026-09-14-snapback4-clique-0.2.10/README.md)
retains all phase outcomes, failed attempts and protocol deviations. Received
holistic scores are Astra 82, Grok 63 and Muse 82 against 84/70/80 on 0.2.6;
Astra's separate 0.2.9 calibration was 85. The independent 119-ID totals are
99/2/18, 71/30/18 and 98/3/18 (pass/fail/not-run). They are not comparable
completion-adjusted gains: native classification changed, Grok stopped early,
and grading/scale methods and coverage differ. N=1 per family and pin is
calibration, not a magnitude or causal claim. The ceremony bar separately
shows the self-witnessing membership rule retained in all three 0.2.10 runs
with zero Snapback-attributed loops; that local absorption does not settle
the full-app reporting question.

This is not a fully compliant docs-only cell: the bounded public-request audits
find packaged implementation reads in Grok and Muse, and their correction
records do not supply comparable normalized loop counts. The record therefore
does not claim that the declared full-cell loop-bar gate passed. The concrete
reporting observations below support a qualified recommendation, with those
protocol limits left visible.

Reporting still costs correction work after §5. Astra's M8 log records a
reporting/ranking residency correction to preserve social-write prediction,
then removal of an invalid explicit sync override on a maintain. Grok's daily
counter helper read before ordinary insert/update and required an authority
correction. Both are received agent-error authoring loops, not newly confirmed
engine defects; the bundled fixture/UI repairs are separate. The independently
confirmed presence-guide mismatch cost an integration loop, and the later §19
fix receives no credit at this pin. Retained aggregation/polling iterations
and final work tables show additional integration work, but the three ledgers
are not normalized or equivalent-coverage counts. Accepted upsert behavior,
unconfirmed broad client diagnoses, smaller source size and unrelated app bugs
are not substituted for those concrete reporting events.

**Recommendation recorded 2026-09-15 (admitted 2026-09-16 below):** give §9.1 application SQL reads their
Moves line on the remaining reporting cost shown here. This reads “reporting still costs
loops” as remaining reporting-authoring cost, including the two retained
agent-error corrections above. It does not claim a confirmed report-specific
engine defect, or prove that SQL uniquely eliminates either loop. Requiring
that proof would add a bar the rerun protocol did not state. A stricter reading
that imports the ceremony bar's Snapback-only attribution would not establish
the same result; the record makes that distinction explicit. The proposed
scan planner, second authorization translation and approximate SQLite VM
meter still need the author's judgment. No Moves line or SQL implementation
changes in this recording landing. Worker actions are already built; none of
these three apps used an action, so this cell does not measure their
sufficiency. The four §14 rulings accepted on September 14 remain settled.

**2026-09-16 author ruling:** Charlie admitted application SQL reads.
[LLP 3000.009 §6.2 and §14.4](./3000.009-general-purpose-backend.rfc.md#62-sql-reads)
govern their authorized-input form on both stores; implementation remains
increment D. The corresponding Moves line is in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`.
This decision does not revise the measurement limits above or claim SQL's
implementation, qualification or publication.

The three fresh M7 sessions ended with received green outcomes in
2,870.258 / 1,667.198 / 895.247 s. These are builder results, not new independent
grades. Astra backfilled a message contribution column feeding a persisted sum;
Grok added a maintained count in a fresh in-memory store; Muse kept its
inherited maintain and added a capped read-time count, with no count backfill.
They do not establish equivalent forced migrations. Snapshot field/state,
receipt/source ordering and native-proof limits remain in the record. Astra's
separate [maintained-ID adoption defect](../issues/20260915-snapback4-maintained-identity-blocks-additive-schema-adoption.md)
is reproduced on the published pin and ticketed; it is an engineering defect,
not an additional claim that SQL is necessary.

## 11. Alternatives considered

- **A relaxed grade by syntax** (`query x(): server` admits the relaxed
  forms; device programs keep the strict ones). Rejected: §3.2 shows the
  forms are device-safe, so the grade would exist only to preserve a
  restriction the meter already replaced, and every author would learn
  two languages to write one.
- **Online-only by default; `sync` as the opt-in exception.** Rejected:
  it is already the case (a table without `sync` is online-only) and the
  bet of LLP 2000.000 §7 is local-first; §7 paves the choice without
  moving the default.
- **Make the residency line mandatory now.** Deferred to the author; the
  table view gives the same legibility without a line on every table.
- **`{ unavailable: "requires-online" }` on the card.** Not needed:
  `denied: E_OFFLINE`, family `link`, retryable, is that word, and §6.5
  makes it the only offline outcome of a server-run read that has no
  last answer.
- **A typed `map`** (`m[k] = v`, `keys`, `has`; `Value::Map` exists in
  the value model). Deferred: it needs a typing rule and a `SetKey`
  statement in the IR; `group` covers every grouped shape Clique asks
  for; the ceremony bar says whether the general tool is still wanted.
- **Acquired history**: rows the server answered to a device-run query
  that fell back past its horizon are kept on the device as delivered
  history for that group, so "previously viewed" survives a cold start
  without a wide horizon. The 0.2.6 lane removed its horizons to satisfy
  the brief's "previously synced" and paid the cold sync. Deferred: the
  replica would hold a second kind of row, shaped queries cannot supply
  rows, and revocation must cover both kinds; a real feature for
  messengers, with a substrate cost this document does not price.
- **World-readable counters** as the idiom for cross-principal writes.
  Rejected: it is what two lanes did, and it is a disclosure; §8.7 is
  the mechanism.
- **A full `fn`/`proc` unification with recursion.** Recursion stays
  refused (an unbounded loop by another name); inline expansion with a
  depth of eight is kept.

## 12. Implementation sequence

**A0 — the meter** (`crates/snapback4-core`): §5.0, measured on the
fixture, with its corpus stress tests; nothing in A lands before it.

**A — the language** (`crates/snapback4-lang`, `crates/snapback4-core`,
the guide): §5.1's seven; `fn` bodies and `proc` deleted; job preludes;
range seeks and the `where`-to-seek note; `group`, `first`, `last`,
`at`, `count(text)`, `lower`; the page-list unification; `Scan.filter`
with §5.6's cursor rule in `read.rs` and `pages.rs`; `upsert` patching;
column defaults; the aliases (on the author's ruling); the language card
and the README's §2 rewritten; the "left out" list cut; the refused
sites and their tests deleted; the corpus gains the new forms;
`tests/clique.rs` gains the §5.8 programs compiled and run.

**B — residency and sync** (`snapback4-lang` binder, `emit.rs`,
`crates/snapback4` verbs, the client): the two words per operation;
`online only` on tables and maintains; the inferred audience;
`N_MAINTAIN_SYNCED`, `N_NOW_SERVER`, `N_NOT_PREDICTABLE`; `check
--residency` and `test --residency`; `why` for operations and tables;
`api.ts` `runs`/`predicts` and the doc comments; `local.ts`'s
last-answer memory and server-run liveness (§6.5), and the removal of
its runtime `onlineOnly` test in favour of the compiled word.

**C — the seams** (`verbs.rs`, `contract.ts`, `local.ts`, `react.tsx`,
`web.ts`, the device, `serve.rs`): §8.1–8.6, §8.8–8.11, §8.13; the card
change of §8.1 lands only on the author's ruling; §8.7 and §5.9 land only
after the blind security review returns. The received review's order —
close the mutation-result, prediction and offline-startup gaps with the
language, before residency — is adopted: §8.1–8.4 land with A, and so
does §8.13, the media cache, by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13.

**C2 — worker actions** (§9.2; the wire, `snapback4/worker`, `dev`
spawning `snapback/worker.ts`, the generated `Action<A, R>`): commissioned
by the ruling of 2026-09-13; designed against Interview's authentication
provider and model calls; lands after C's seams.

**D — the recipes and the modes**: §6.9's two cards; `init --online`
and `init --expo`; the README's "Two ways to run."

**E — the bars and the rerun** (§10.3): the ceremony bar authored and
run on the current pin before A lands, so it has a before; the rerun on
the pin after A–D; then the ruling on §9.1, SQL reads, following measurement
of the reporting gap. That ruling was received on 2026-09-16 (§10.3;
LLP 3000.009 §14.4), with implementation remaining in LLP 3000.009's D.

A0 precedes A; A and B are independent; C's §8.1–8.4 and §8.13 travel with A and
the rest is independent except that §8.7 and §5.9 wait on their review;
D follows A and B; E's "before" runs first.

## 13. Acceptance

1. Every ```quarry block on the language card compiles and runs with
   §5's forms present; the "left out on purpose" list has no entry the
   parser accepts.
2. The §5.8 programs run in `tests/clique.rs` against the fixture and
   produce the same values as their 0.1.0 shapes; the §5.2 receipt helper
   agrees with the 0.2.6 lane's three-helper form on the fixture.
3. A filtered page cut by the meter continues past the rejected run on
   the next call (a fixture with more rejected rows than the examined
   ceiling), and a filled page's cursor is after its last returned row;
   a seek on the first unpinned column examines only the rows in range.
4. `check --residency` on the retained 0.2.6 Clique schemas prints a
   class for every operation, and `api.ts` carries it; the 0.2.6 Astra
   `send` with its redundant guard produces `N_NOT_PREDICTABLE` naming
   the guard; the repost counter produces `N_MAINTAIN_SYNCED` with the
   fixture's row count.
5. A synced table with `sync` and no `to` clause compiles to the audience
   its read rule states for each of the three rule forms; a `to` clause
   that disagrees with the rule refuses as today; the Muse lane's schema
   compiles with its six `E_SYNC` iterations replayed as one edit.
6. Under §8.7, an `upsert` into another principal's row admitted by the
   update rule succeeds and returns no row; the same write under a
   refusing update rule is `E_RULE`; an `insert … if absent` against a
   hidden row is `E_RULE`; the simulator's disclosure invariant (LLP 3000
   §6, no row renders that the viewer may not read) holds across the
   change, and the blind review finds no new channel.
7. A server-run query with no link answers the last server answer with
   `fresh: false` and `since`, or `denied: E_OFFLINE` when there is none,
   never an empty page; an observed server-run query on the local-first
   client re-runs when a commit touches its tables.
8. Online-only rows are absent from every device persistence and stream
   witness (from LLP 3000.006); the compact receipt recipe on the fixture
   holds one row per member per conversation and its aggregate matches
   the broadcast form's.
9. `snapback4 init --online`, `check`, `dev` and the online client run
   the template's operations live across two clients; `init --expo`
   exports an iOS Hermes bundle on the pinned toolchain.
10. `useMutation().run()` resolves with the server's result; a shaped
    row this device wrote carries `pending`; an offline reload opens the
    wasm device with no network; the `{ id }` video argument predicts;
    `check --fix` rewrites the three mechanical classes and leaves the
    rest; `data --sql` answers a `SELECT` over a generated view and
    refuses a write.
11. The ceremony bar has a before on the current pin and an after on the
    landing's pin, N ≥ 3 each; the rerun of §10.3 completes on every
    family without a Snapback-attributed capability failure; the loop
    bar holds.
12. §5.0: a concatenation loop and a list-growing loop abort with
    `E_BOUND` naming the heap meter at the ceiling on the server and on
    both device stores; the fixture's programs measure no more than
    before the change.
13. §5.6: on a non-uniform range, no cursor the server or a device mints
    decodes to a hidden row's key values, under a simulator check that
    decodes every cursor a run produced against the viewer's read
    verdicts; a cut page on a uniform range continues past a rejected
    run longer than the examined ceiling.
14. §5.3: the corrected Trending query reports `complete: false` on a
    fixture day with more posts than the examined ceiling and the top
    ten of the whole day otherwise; §6.2: `api.ts` for the 0.2.6 Astra
    schema lists `send`'s `exists messages[id]` as a `horizon` site.
15. The review's measures, recorded before and after on the retained
    0.2.6 schemas and the rerun: fewer work tables, fewer auxiliary
    queries, less client lifecycle code, correct complete answers, and
    the acquisition of §6.8.

## 14. The author's rulings (2026-09-13)

Each question as it was asked, and the ruling.

1. Accept §5.1's seven forms whole, or a subset first? **Ruled: all seven,
   after the meter fix of §5.0.**
2. §5.2–§5.4 — `fn` bodies with `proc` deleted; job preludes; range seeks;
   `group`/`first`/`last`/`at`; `upsert` patching; column defaults. **Ruled:
   yes to all**, with the note that any of them found to cause problems
   can come back out.
3. §5.5, the guessed spellings, against "one spelling". **Ruled: the
   language stays one spelling.** No alias is admitted; the diagnostic
   names the canonical form for each guess and `check --fix` applies it.
   Agents will learn the language; the tool makes the fix cheap.
4. §5.6: reads inside `where` from the start? **Ruled: yes.**
5. §6.6: infer the audience from the read rule, `to` optional? **Ruled:
   yes.**
6. §8.7: commission the blind security review of write authority?
   **Ruled: yes.** Commissioned and returned 2026-09-13 (§15): GPT NOT
   READY, Grok READY WITH CHANGES; §8.7 rewritten; ruling 15 opened.
7. `online only`: optional or required? **Ruled: optional now; revisit
   after the rerun.**
8. The card: `result` on `sent` and `predicted` on `pending`? **Ruled:
   `result` on `sent` only.** `predicted` is not added; a prediction is
   visible as `pending` rows, not as an answer.
9. `dev`'s identity limits on loopback? **Ruled: as high as the server can
   carry right now.** Under `dev` on loopback the per-peer and per-email
   minute counters are lifted; the Argon2 slots and the connection caps,
   which are the host's physical bounds, remain; `serve` is unchanged.
   Applied 2026-09-14: `dev` loopback Argon2 slots use the host's available
   parallelism, capped at 16 and never below 2, and startup prints the count;
   `serve` keeps two slots and saturation remains retryable `E_RATE_LIMIT`.
10. `sql` inspection against `NOT-DOING.md`'s observability line? **Ruled:
    observability extends to a fifth named verb, `snapback4 sql`,** and
    the Moves line is written (`snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`, Moves,
    2026-09-13).
11. §9: rule after the rerun, or commission now? **Ruled: the lead's
    recommendation** — SQL reads wait for the reporting gap to be measured
    after §5; worker actions are commissioned with the seams (§12, C2),
    because Interview already runs on Snapback 4 and needs them.
    **2026-09-16 follow-up:** Charlie admits application SQL reads in
    LLP 3000.009 §6.2's form; its §14.4 records the ruling.
12. LLP 3000.006 superseded? **Ruled: keep it superseded.**
13. §5.9: the non-predicting mutation's page pass — same blind review as
    §8.7? **Ruled: yes**, bundled into the review of ruling 6; returned
    2026-09-13 (§15); §5.9 rewritten to uniform prefixes only.
14. §8.13: the SDK media cache? **Ruled: with the language landing.**
15. §8.7 and §5.9 as revised after the blind review (§15): accept for
    implementation as written, narrow further, or drop? **Ruled
    2026-09-13: option 1** — build both as revised, with §15's regressions
    as the acceptance and a second blind pass by the same two families on
    the implementation before it lands.

16. §9.2's card word for a worker action, landed with the actions
    (2026-09-13) under ruling 11: `Act<R>` is `done` (the worker's
    result), `failed` (admitted and run; the worker's refusal), `pending`
    (recorded but not settled inside the wait; collect it again by id) or
    `{denied, id}` (refused before admission — the rule, or `E_OFFLINE`,
    since an action is never predicted or queued on a device). **Open:**
    accept the four words as landed, or fold the shape into `Write<R>`'s
    (`sent`/`pending`/`denied`) with `failed` added. The lead's
    recommendation: accept as landed — an action has an outcome a
    mutation cannot have (admitted, run, and failed), `done` says the
    worker finished rather than that the server accepted, and `pending`
    keeps one meaning on both cards: not settled yet, ask again.

17. The second blind pass on the implementation of §8.7 and §5.9 (§17)
    returned NOT READY (GPT) and READY WITH CHANGES (Grok). Three of its
    findings are questions the contract left open, so they are rulings,
    each built in the conservative direction by lane W2 until ruled:
    **(a)** an immutable column in a patch on an unreadable target: today
    a matching guess is `sent` and a mismatch `NOT_FOUND`, a bit-by-bit
    search of the hidden value. Recommendation: refuse the patch
    `NOT_FOUND` without comparing, whatever the guess; a readable target
    keeps its named `E_CONSTRAINT`. **(b)** an author condition
    (`update t[key] { … } if .col = expected`) on an unreadable target is
    the same search: success means the condition held. Recommendation:
    refuse conditions on an unreadable target `NOT_FOUND` without
    evaluating them — §8.7 grants that an admitted row exists and
    nothing more, and a blind counter needs no condition (the engine
    merges `old`). **(c)** a mutation that reads a page over a
    non-uniform prefix without looping can still hit the meter's abort,
    the oracle §5.9's narrowing closed for loops. Recommendation: the
    same rule for every page a mutation reads — uniformly decided by
    the prefix, or refused at `check` with the rewrite "read it in a
    query, or pin the deciding columns"; the lane reports how many
    in-repo programs that refuses. Each is a narrowing; the author may
    relax any of them after W2 lands. **Open.**
    *Note after the third pass (2026-09-14):* for (a) the lead's
    recommendation moves from refusing the patch to ignoring its immutable
    columns on an unreadable target, because the refusal reopened an
    existence bit on `upsert` (§17, third pass); (b) gains a binder
    refusal of `upsert … if`; (c) is unchanged and refused zero programs.

**2026-09-14 — author ruling on 16 and 17 (a)–(c).** Charlie Cheever
accepted the orchestrator's four recommendations together: "your recs fine."
The earlier questions and review notes above are retained as history; all
four choices are now ruled **accept as landed**:

- **16:** keep `Act<R>` as `done`, `failed`, `pending`, or `{denied, id}`,
  with the meanings recorded above.
- **17 (a):** on a target the writer cannot read, ignore immutable patch
  columns without comparing them, on both `update` and `upsert`. A readable
  immutable mismatch keeps its named refusal.
- **17 (b):** refuse an author condition on an unreadable target with
  `NOT_FOUND`, without evaluation; refuse `upsert … if` at bind/check.
- **17 (c):** every page read by a mutation must be uniformly decided by
  its pinned prefix and use a plain prefix scan; `matching`, merge and
  expiring-table page reads remain refused at `check`. The in-repo census
  recorded in §17 refused zero programs.

18. Presence in a mutation (§19.1). The presence card said a prediction
    reads everyone as connected, and the runtime does that; the binder
    classified every `connected[...]` as a server site, so the card's own
    `send` recipe could never predict. Two builders (0.2.10 and 0.2.12)
    paid a loop for it. **2026-09-14 — ruled: option C.** Presence may
    decide what a mutation emits without costing prediction; presence that
    decides rows, a refusal, the result or control flow over writes keeps
    the mutation `predicts: never`.

19. `upsert` predicts its insert when absence is provable (§20.5).
    **2026-09-14 — ruled: yes, as recommended.**
20. A read rule may name a service principal (§20.6). **2026-09-14 — ruled:
    yes, as recommended.**
21. A public table may sync a subset to each device (§20.7). **2026-09-14 —
    ruled: yes, as recommended, limited to tables whose read rule is
    `public`, landed after wave 2.**
22. The published darwin binary is signed with the Developer ID identity
    (§20.8). **2026-09-14 — ruled: "yes do it."**
23. A query's lane source and prefix loop are bounded by the meter (§20.22).
    **2026-09-17 — ruled: yes, both** — a merge lane source and a query `for`
    over a uniformly decided prefix may omit `first N`; mutations and jobs are
    unchanged, and a returned page still needs `first N` or a cap.
24. A capped group needs no restated bound (§20.22). **2026-09-17 — ruled: yes;
    `first <cap>` stays legal**, with no note and no forced rewrite.
25. `exists` takes a prefix read or a `matching` read in program expressions
    (§20.22). **2026-09-17 — ruled: admit it**; rule probes are unchanged.
26. A cut uniform merge continues on one cursor (§20.22.5). **2026-09-17 —
    ruled: commission the design and its blind review now**; nothing lands
    before both families read READY. Fixed points: the uniform class only; one
    cursor and no new `Read` field; ordered windows past one read's budget,
    each page of such a walk `complete: false, cut: 'meter'` until the last;
    `usePage` treats a followed `next` as discharging that page's cut; the
    two-cursor card section stays until this lands. **Amended the same day,
    after two review rounds: the windowed continuation (Part O) is shelved —
    it did not converge and the grouped one-cursor search already continues
    after a cut; the fifth fixed point is re-ruled — the two-cursor section,
    `init --recipe search`, `src/search.ts` and `searchChats` are replaced by
    the grouped form in the landing that carries §20.22.1 item 1 and ruling
    25; the drain-cut continuation (Part D) lands on READY from both
    families.**
27. Ruling 21's timing (§20.7, §20.22). **2026-09-17 — ruled: it stays ruled
    yes and is decided on measured residency** — after the landings above, with
    per-table cold rows and bytes for the reference app taken first.
28. A merge lane source may be `range(a, b)` or a bounded list (§21.3 P3).
    **2026-09-17 — ruled: yes, both**, in queries, bounded by the meter. Each
    lane is the usual lane scan with its own verdict; there is no static lane
    count, so `check --cost` prints "lanes ≤ meter" and N_WIDE_MERGE does not
    fire, as for ruling 23's boundless prefix lane source; mutations and jobs
    are unchanged.
29. A table index may lead with `day`, `hour` or `bucket` of a required time
    column (§21.3 P3). **2026-09-17 — ruled: yes, as index keys only** — the
    three existing functions in one more position, pinned positionally by a
    read; no computed column and no new card word; immutability is required
    only where an index already needs it (a job sweep's cursor index).
30. Maintained columns — an aggregate on the row it describes (§21.3 P2).
    **2026-09-17 — ruled: yes in direction; commission the design and its
    two-family blind review now; nothing is built before READY from both.**
    First scope: the group is a reference to this table, the source does not
    expire, the cascade is acyclic. The design states the per-column writer
    class it introduces and the stream cost of a hot row.
    **READY from both families on 2026-09-17**, at revision 5 of
    [LLP 3000.007.001](./3000.007.001-maintained-columns.rfc.md) after five
    blind rounds (its §10); the first build has no cascade. Acceptance is the
    author's; that design's §7 gates are measured before a lane is briefed.
31. `N_PUBLIC_AGGREGATE_COPY` generalised to a column derived in fact under a
    rule that admits everyone (§21.3 P5). **2026-09-17 — ruled: yes, after a
    false-positive census** over every card, recipe, template and the retained
    calibration schemas, asserted in a test; a note, never an error; its two
    rewrites are the maintain (or ruling 30's column) and §21.1 item 3's rule.
32. Presence defers an effect and never drops one (§21.3 P6). **2026-09-17 —
    ruled: yes; measure the effect-row cost on the chat fixture first.** When
    presence decides only effects, a poll held at commit is a short grace, not
    proof; only that principal's poll arriving after the commit satisfies the
    effect; "a live poll at claim" stops satisfying; no poll and no grace
    stays immediate. The storage shape follows the measurement.
33. A mutation page over a table with expiry, when its prefix is uniformly
    decided (§21.3 P4). **2026-09-17 — ruled: commission the blind review;
    land only on READY from both families.** Non-uniform prefixes, `matching`
    and merges stay refused.
34. `group`, `sort` and the other row-preserving helpers over a page (§20.22.7,
    the `group-drops-next/` dead end every bar builder met). **2026-09-18 —
    ruled, on the lead's recommendation: carry the page's continuation
    through a row-preserving helper.** A query that returns `group(page, …)`
    or `sort(page, …)` publishes the page's `next` as if it returned the page;
    a helper that drops or reorders elements does not. A `check` note is not
    the remedy. Lane W13i, two-family blind review.
35. A returned shape that cannot finish under any fresh meter (§20.22.7; the
    ticket W13h filed). **2026-09-18 — ruled: a runtime refusal on the
    no-progress follow.** A request that begins at an inclusive cursor and
    starves that same first row again refuses with a registered code whose
    card section names the shape and the ceiling, instead of returning the
    cursor it was given; a `check --cost` note for a returned shape whose own
    bound exceeds the ceiling follows when the cost arithmetic lands. Lane
    W13j, two-family blind review.
36. The horizon acquisition's selection time (§20.22.7; §18.2). **2026-09-18
    — ruled: the acquisition is selected at the current state, over readable
    rows only.** The stream serves the current top-N readable rows of the
    group at the time it serves the join, so the boundary is the Nth delivered
    key by construction and no completion proof consults history; §18.2's "a
    join acquires the images that existed at its own commit" is amended to
    the current readable images at the time the stream serves the join. It
    changes retained-history law, so it goes through a design amendment with
    two blind reviews before a lane (W13k); W13g's `join_completion` proof and
    `written_after` are deleted by that lane.
37. A runtime ownership rule for starved shapes beyond the canonical form
    (§20.22.7; the ticket). **2026-09-18 — ruled: deferred to the bounds bar's
    "after".** If non-canonical returned shapes appear in what builders write,
    a design with two blind reviews first — the runner knows what it returned.
38. `held` with `E_QUERY_HELD`, and the feeds recipe as the default rewrite for
    a wide Following merge (§20.22.7). **2026-09-18 — ruled: deferred to the
    bar's "after" and the keys-only measurement**; neither changes in wave 13's
    pin. Wave 14 is not commissioned before the "after" is measured; if the bar
    and the calibration do not move, the next wave removes surface rather than
    documenting it.

On 2026-09-17 the author also delegated, for about twelve hours, the
ruling-sized decisions that arise while this work proceeds ("make decisions
based on your recommendations … use your best judgement"). Decisions taken
under that delegation are marked as the lead's in §21.8, never as the
author's, and none of them publishes a package.

This accepts the existing guarded implementations; it commissions no
relaxation or code change. Ruling 11's later SQL-read admission is recorded
above (2026-09-16), following §10.3's qualified reporting finding.

This document is therefore Accepted as ruled, §8.7 and §5.9 included
as revised in §15; until A0–E run, LLP 3000 §7's current decisions bind
and the language card is the language.

### Rulings 39–40 (2026-09-18)

**Ruling 39 — the maintained-columns design is accepted and built now; the
value-index tie-break is not relaxed.** The open question was whether to widen a
maintain value index to *a prefix of group fields, then `value`, then any
remaining group fields, then `id`* (option 1 of
`issues/20260917-maintain-value-index-cannot-tie-by-a-group-column.md`, the
derived-data lead's recommendation), so that a maintained ranking could tie
newest-first. The author ruled instead for **LLP 3000.007.001**, which is hereby
**Accepted**: an aggregate shown as a column of the row it describes
(`likeCount: count likes by postId`) makes the ranking an ordinary index on the
parent — `by byLikes: likeCount, at, id` — whose ties are whatever the author
writes. The limit is not relaxed; it is retired along with the form that has it.

The reasoning is the one §20.23 records. Option 1 restates the index rule as a
longer rule; the accepted design deletes a concept instead, and §20.20's caveats
with it. Option 2 stays declined. Option 3 — document the limit — is what has
shipped and is what three consecutive builders answered by hand-keeping a
`posts.score` with `update <- allow`, which is the security shape wave 12 had to
detect. A design that removes the reason to write that is worth more than advice
against writing it.

Sequencing is unchanged by acceptance and is stated so it is not lost: gate 5 is
the lane's milestone zero, and
`issues/20260917-snapback4-oversized-sync-page-hides-generation.md` is a
prerequisite of the first build (§3.11) and was **still open at this ruling**;
MC0's implementation and proof are recorded in §18.2 and the
[closed ticket](../issues/closed/20260917-snapback4-oversized-sync-page-hides-generation.md).
Gates 1 and 2 are
measured and pass (gate 2 at 0.0% on main after wave 13's horizon repair).
First scope holds: the group is a reference to this table, the source does not
expire, no cascade.

**Ruling 40 — documentation is not the default repair; "remove surface" is not
yet policy.** §14's earlier condition ("if the bar and the calibration do not
move, the next wave removes surface rather than documenting it") posed a binary
that the evidence of 2026-09-18 does not fit. Six defects were found and fixed
that day from one calibration's evidence, and **none was repaired by adding
documentation and none by removing a feature** — every one was repaired by making
a message that already fired say the right thing (§20.23). That is a third
answer the binary did not name.

Ruled: when a builder gets something wrong, the first question is what the tool
*told* them and whether it could have told them the repair. Documentation is the
answer only when no diagnostic could have carried it. Removal stays available
and is to be tested against one named candidate with a measurement, not adopted
as a wave-wide policy.

This ruling is explicitly provisional. The surface budget moved the wrong way on
the bar that closed §20.22.7 (+320 README lines, +249 card lines), which is
evidence *for* removal that advice quality does not answer: better advice does
not shorten what a builder reads. Calibration #13 is the outstanding evidence,
and this ruling is to be revisited on its score — a recovery toward 90 reads as
the decline having been noise, and a third consecutive fall at or below 85 earns
the removal wave.

#### Ruling 40's revisit (2026-09-18, calibration #13 on 0.2.29)

**The condition above was underspecified and the result landed in its gap.** #13
scored holistic **89** — neither the "toward 90" that would read as noise nor the
"at or below 85" that would earn the removal wave. That is a defect in the
ruling, not in the run, and it is recorded rather than quietly re-interpreted.

What the series says, fitted over the eleven comparable runs (independently
reproduced here and by the wave-13 session's `tools/cal-series.py`, which also
reproduces the earlier ten-run numbers exactly): mean **89.09**, sd **1.81**,
slope **−0.227/run**, drift **−2.27**, still beyond one sd. **89 is 0.09 below
the mean: it is the series mean, not a move above it.** The decline persists and
has softened (−0.30 → −0.23). Neither branch of the condition fires.

Three readings the categories force, which the holistic number hides:

- **`setup_and_docs` rose 88 → 92** on a release that removed no surface. 0.2.29
  is wave 13, a capability wave. Its drift is still −3.55 against sd 2.81, so the
  category is still falling across the series — but the largest single-category
  recovery in the programme came from capability, not removal. That is evidence
  against surface *volume* being the cause of the `setup_and_docs` decline, and
  it is the strongest argument yet available against a removal wave.
- **`queries_and_live` 78 is the series low**, declining −1.04/run, now the worst
  category. The defect named is the 32-word first-term search expansion bound —
  **named twice independently**, by #13's grader and by every bounds-bar builder.
  It is a capability limit. Relaxing it adds capability; it is not a removal.
- **`scale_and_limits` 65 → 83** confirms #12's 65 as an outlier, as §20.23's
  cold-acquisition measurement argued it was (host contamination and an app
  residency choice, not a query-performance result). The series should not carry
  65 as a trend point.

**Refined condition, replacing the binary above.** The holistic score is too
coarse to rule on: its sd is 1.81 and a wave moves it by less. Judge the two
categories the surface hypothesis actually predicts — `setup_and_docs` and
`queries_and_live` — against their own fitted drift, and require the removal wave
to name what it removes and predict the category it moves *before* it runs.

**And the reason #13 cannot test this ruling at all:** the six advice repairs of
§20.23 landed on main *after* 0.2.29 was published, so #13's builder never saw
one of them. Calibration #14, on the 0.2.30 pin, is the first run that tests
advice-first. Ruling 40 stands unrevised until then, on the evidence that it has
not yet been tested rather than on evidence that it worked.

### Rulings 41–44 (2026-09-18, walked through with the derived-data lead)

The author walked the derived-data program's open decisions with its lead
(§21.8 L1–L25) and ruled as follows. Where a ruling touches an earlier one it
says so; nothing here publishes a package.

**Ruling 41 — the maintain value-index tie-break IS relaxed, as an interim;
this overturns the second sentence of ruling 39 and nothing else in it.** A
maintain value index is *a prefix of group fields, then `value`, then any
remaining group fields, then `id`* (option 1 of
`issues/20260917-maintain-value-index-cannot-tie-by-a-group-column.md`), so a
maintained ranking can tie newest-first before maintained columns ship. The
lead put ruling 39's reasoning in front of the author and recommended keeping it
— the column form retires the limit, and the relaxation is surface that becomes
redundant within days — and the author chose the interim anyway: lane **W15c**,
a language-surface change with a two-family blind review before it lands.
Ruling 39's acceptance of LLP 3000.007.001 stands; that design's status line is
amended to say the tie-break was later relaxed for the interim.

*Addendum, the same day, on the wave 12 lead's flag:* **the relaxation is explicitly
temporary and is retired when maintained columns land.** Removing it is a task of the
MC1 landing — the refusal returns, pointing at the column form
(`likeCount: count likes by postId` and an ordinary parent index) — so that the interim
never becomes load-bearing by default and the surface budget ruling 40 measures is not
charged for it permanently. W15c's card text says the rule is interim.

**Ruling 39 reaffirmed — maintained columns are built now**, with the sequence
the design states: after 0.2.30, the activation prototype (gate 5: the private
rebuild, the in-place mirror pass, a real device adopting 100,000 parents over
HTTP) is the lane's milestone zero with a stop if it fails; two-family blind
review before landing. Milestone 0 in the other sense — the oversized-sync-page
prerequisite — is the wave 12 lead's MC0, shipped in 0.2.30; ownership of the
column itself is settled between the two leads before a lane is briefed, so that
one lane, not two, builds it.

**Ruling 42 — ruling 31's lane (W15b, the provenance census for
`N_PUBLIC_AGGREGATE_COPY`) runs after W15c lands**, not before and not in
parallel: its rewrite advice — keep the count in a maintain — is followable for a
ranking only once the tie-break exists.

**Ruling 43 — decisions L1–L25 stand.** The behavioural ones the author saw
before ruling: ruling 33 stays declined (L10); the presence semantics of ruling
32 as built (L16–L23: a guarded push is always recorded; a connected-looking
recipient is hedged; the paved form charges what the graced case charged).

**Ruling 44 — the three unowned tickets go to the derived-data program.**
`issues/20260917-snapback4-every-commit-wakes-every-held-poll.md` (P2) and
`issues/20260917-snapback4-stream-page-serves-images-newer-than-its-watermark.md`
(P2) are lanes after W15c and the maintained-columns build, the second only once
wave 13 has left `partition.rs`; the reservation gap
(`issues/20260917-snapback4-presence-conjunction-server-charges-more-heap-than-prediction-reserves.md`,
P3) when convenient. Same rails: one lane at a time, a two-family review, no
publish.

### Rulings 45–51 (2026-09-22, taken by the 0.3 program lead under the author's delegation)

The author asked for LLP 3000.012's plan to be carried out in Snapback 4, on
the release line **0.3.x** ("do your new plan"; "snapback4 0.3.x is fine"). He
delegated the decisions that plan named while he was away ("try to make good
decisions without waiting for me"). Those decisions are LLP 3000.007.003 §11
and LLP 3000.009.000 §7. Each ruling below is the lead's, taken with that
plan's recommended default, and recorded so the author can overturn it.
None of them publishes a package or raises a ceiling.

**Ruling 45 — §21.7 narrows** (LLP 3000.007.003 §1). The admission rule's five
conditions are accepted in direction. Join views stay out, with two admitted
cases:

- engine-recorded attribution through immutable references to public targets
  (F2);
- the Following recipe's capped timeline, as a declaration (F3).

General view maintenance stays out.

**Ruling 46 — F2 is engine-recorded attribution** (LLP 3000.007.003 §3.2).

- The engine records the target facts on the source row at contribution time.
- The recorded fields are engine-internal: absent from generated types and from
  `*`, and visible to `sql` and `why`.
- First scope: public targets, one hop, an immutable reference and immutable
  target columns.
- An unresolved reference records null. It never refuses.
- Recorded values are never re-resolved. Adding a path to a populated table
  is a once-only, activation-time capture with the stated semantics of a
  hand-written backfill, including target id reuse. Activation publishes no
  row events.
- The fields are guarded against every program write, with `Engine::load`
  split into trusted restore and untrusted import. They are nullable, and a
  calendar key over a null recorded time yields the null group. The full
  contract is in LLP 3000.007.003 §3.2, revision 3.

**Ruling 47 — F3 preserves the recipe's contract exactly in its first scope**
(LLP 3000.007.003 §3.3, revision 3). That includes its durable engine-owned
state, which is never rebuilt, a declared one-way removal signal, and activation
only over empty tables. Changed semantics, such as full history or reversible
promotion, are not commissioned. F3's lane starts after a focused review of
§3.3.

**Ruling 48 — MC1's gate is not revised.** Generation discovery before a sync
page is materialized is commissioned. Gate 5 is re-measured, unchanged, after it
lands. Each derived form bounds and tests its largest current-generation commit
when it lands.

**Ruling 49 — `maintain` is the one keyword** for counts, maintained columns and
timelines.

**Ruling 50 — implementation.** The program lead acts as core owner and
integrates Astra xhigh implementation lanes. Each core lane gets a two-family
blind review, from Astra max and Grok xhigh, before it lands. F3 also gets
decision 4's partition review. Maintained columns are built under ruling 39's
sequence, and their landing retires ruling 41's interim, as ruling 41's
addendum requires.

**Ruling 51 — packaging** (LLP 3000.009.000 §7).

- There is one `use server` directive.
- Native jobs and the witness verbs stay core.
- Application SQL and Postgres will share `use server` when they ship.

Two families reviewed the two proposals blind, read-only, at

on the same channels and on the same repair, which §8.7 and §5.9 now
carry; the table records each finding's disposition.

| Finding | Family | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The baseline does not universally require read permission for updates: by-id `update` already resolves without a read check; the binder's unique-key path is what Clique hits | both | Accepted; §8.7 now names the binder path as the change and by-id as the starting point (a) |
| Admission and success are different predicates: `NOT_FOUND` vs `E_RULE` vs `E_CONFLICT` vs incomplete-upsert `E_INPUT` vs a named immutable mismatch distinguish absence, refusal, condition and content | both (GPT 1, Grok 1) | Accepted; one failure word for an unreadable target (b); the rule's own predicates are the disclosure granted |
| The merged patch image, `old`, `result` and `current` must never reach the author's program or the wire; asset-fact validation runs before the update rule and can name a foreign asset id | GPT 3, Grok 1.5/4 | Accepted (c) |
| Withhold before `_ops` records; replay returns cached `result`/`current` after revocation today | GPT 5, Grok 2 | Accepted (d); the existing gap is a P1 ticket |
| The device predicts `NOT_FOUND` or a pending insert for a target it does not hold; `admit` fails on any refusal but `E_PREDICT`; §6.2's `held` class is a read class | GPT 6, Grok 2 | Accepted (e); lane R's residency work gains the runtime rule as a follow-up |
| Secondary hidden collisions, caps counted physically, `after insert` on the insert branch, budget outcomes near a ceiling, readable maintains over hidden sources | GPT 2, Grok 6 | Accepted in part: collisions with other hidden rows keep S05 (f); a readable maintain is a declared read channel with a `check` note (g); caps, postconditions and budgets are recorded as residuals the narrowed contract does not claim to close |
| §5.9 reopens the meter-cut mutation oracle: abort versus success at the examined ceiling on a non-uniform prefix, which a query of the same prefix no longer gives | both (GPT 4, Grok 3) | Accepted; §5.9 admits uniform prefixes only; the cap-like-a-query variant is named, not proposed |
| The S05 contract must be revised explicitly, case by case; the kept `insert … if absent` refusal and an admitted upsert are permission-consistent | both | Accepted; S05's cases restated in (f) and in the regression list |

## 16. The ceremony bar's "before" (2026-09-13)

The bar of §10.3 ran three times on the published `snapback4@0.2.8`,
the pin that predates every landing of this document, from the lead's
shell with the instrument lane B built
([record](../benchmarks/runs/2026-09-13-snapback4-ceremonybar-before/README.md)).
All three went green and were independently reverified: 510, 407 and
456 s wall; loops 3, 5 and 3, of which Snapback-attributed 0, 1 and 0;
ten tables each; maintains 8, 1 and 1; a digest guard table in every
run; no world-readable counter, because the brief's digest runs as the
system and the cross-principal counter shape of the Clique lanes does not
arise on this seed.

The finding the bar was built to surface came on the first `check` of
every run: `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` on the membership scan behind the receipt
aggregate and the chat directory, and the same answer from every agent —
a second, private table duplicating the membership relationship keyed
the other way, kept in step by the same mutations. That is ceremony of
the §3.1 R kind: a restatement. A membership row read by the viewer's
own principal (`memberships[viewer] … by byPrincipalChat`) under a rule
`exists memberships[.chatId, viewer]` is its own witness — the row being
scanned is exactly the probe the rule would make — so the page is
complete by construction and the note should not fire. **Candidate,
not commissioned:** the binder recognizes a self-witnessing rule (a scan
whose pinned prefix binds the rule's `viewer` term on the same table)
as uniformly decided, marks the page complete, and drops the note; the
"after" run measures whether the duplicated table disappears. It
belongs with the language work of §5, after L2 lands, and needs no
ruling beyond acceptance of this document.

**2026-09-13 — commissioned as lane S and built.** In an `or`, equality
of the row's column with `viewer` (either operand order) is absorbed only
by an `exists` on the same table's total unique plain key, with `viewer`
at that column and each other key term its own column's field; literals
block it. The engine and binder share the deciding fields. Server roster,
directory, receipt, literal-role, foreign-table and nested-rule regressions
are in `snapback4-sim/tests/membership.rs`; both device stores preserve
online-only coverage and privacy in `snapback4-device/tests/membership/`;
the note's remaining columns are checked in
`snapback4-lang/tests/frontend_regressions.rs` (`self_witness_notes_name_only_the_unpinned_deciding_columns`).

**2026-09-14 — the "after" on `snapback4@0.2.9`** (the pin after the
language landing, before lane S;
[record](../benchmarks/runs/2026-09-13-snapback4-ceremonybar-after/README.md)).
Three green, independently reverified: 494, 403 and 535 s wall; loops
3, 2 and 1 against the before's 3, 5 and 3; Snapback-attributed 0, 0
and 0 against 0, 1 and 0; nine tables each against ten. The tenth table
is gone from every run, and what replaced it sharpens the finding: every
agent made the membership roster public to silence the same note ("so
both principal and chat scans are uniform"), a disclosure in place of a
restatement. The note was wrong both times; lane S's absorption is the
answer, and the bar runs a third time on the pin that carries it.

**2026-09-14 — the third run on `snapback4@0.2.10`** (carrying lane S
and W–W4; [record](../benchmarks/runs/2026-09-14-snapback4-ceremonybar-third/README.md)).
Three green and independently reverified, five of five probes each: 437,
407 and 419 s wall; loops 1, 2 and 1, all agent-error; zero
Snapback-attributed loops; nine source tables each and maintains 1, 1 and
0. Every agent wrote and retained `.principal = viewer or exists
members[.chatKey, viewer]`, with no membership-note loop, duplicate
membership table or public roster. The candidate above is measured:
lane S's absorption removed both observed forms of ceremony on this brief.
N = 3 per pin, calibration only; the pin predates W5, and the shared host
carried the Clique builder from partway through run 1 through runs 2–3.
Runs 1 and 3 still carry a search `N_NO_CURSOR` note. The full Clique
cell supplies §10.3's reporting/integration finding; this bar does not
pre-empt the author's SQL-read ruling.

## 17. The second blind pass: the implementation of §8.7 and §5.9 (2026-09-14)

Ruling 15's condition. Both families reviewed lane W's implementation
blind, read-only, at `32c8bf81b` (the lane rebased onto main at
`30cf4ce80`), on one brief (the contract of §15 as ruled, the twenty-two
regressions, six questions); the artifacts are
[gpt](./reviews/3000.007-write-authority-implementation.gpt.md) and
[grok](./reviews/3000.007-write-authority-implementation.grok.md).
Verdicts: GPT **NOT READY**; Grok **READY WITH CHANGES**. Both find the
contract's core built — engine-private target resolution, one word for an
unreadable target, the merged image kept from `result`, `current` and the
program, the device queueing without a row, S05 kept for inserts, §5.9
uniform-only, `N_MAINTAIN_DELTA`, `delete` untouched, replay withholding
`result` and `current`. They converge on one wrong word and on tests
that promise more than they assert; GPT names four channels beyond that.
Lane W2 builds the dispositions below on top of W, and the implementation
returns to both families once more before it lands.

| Finding | Family | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A secondary hidden unique collision during an update is `NOT_FOUND` even when the target is readable; §8.7 (f) and S05 say `E_RULE` | GPT 5, Grok 1 | Accepted: `E_RULE` (generic, no index, no key) when the target is readable; `NOT_FOUND` when the target is unreadable, which is stricter than (f) and is now written into (f); the regression expects both |
| The absent-target upsert path is not one word with the hidden-refused path: a complete patch naming an unknown or foreign asset raises `E_ASSET_UNKNOWN` before insert authority when the row is absent, `NOT_FOUND` when it is hidden; the hidden path evaluates the read rule's probes and meters the merged image, the absent path neither | GPT 2 | Accepted: on the absent branch the insert rule is decided before any asset or constraint diagnostic, and a refusal is `NOT_FOUND`; the meter is marked before target resolution and restored on every `NOT_FOUND`, so hidden-refused and absent cost the same on every meter field; paired regression with the complete patch and a foreign asset, comparing meters |
| Cap diagnostics print group values from the merged image, so an authorized patch of one column can print a retained hidden column | GPT 3 | Accepted: a cap refusal names the cap's columns and size, never values, on every target |
| The condition purity check accepts unhandled expression forms (`Block`, `Shape`, a scan's filter), so hand-authored IR could write during a condition and copy `old` into a readable row | GPT 3 | Accepted: the traversal is exhaustive with impure as the default, and the engine refuses any write while a condition is being evaluated; serialized-IR regressions attempt writes under a block, a shape and a filter |
| Replay strips `result` and `current` but a refusal recorded in `_ops` keeps row-derived text in `why.message` (a readable collision that named a key), returned after revocation; the log itself stores the original body | GPT 4, Grok 3 | Accepted: the receipt records the replay-shaped body — code, site, rewrite, guide and a message without row-derived detail — so the log never holds readable content and a replay after revocation returns nothing the first reply did not already withhold; the P1 ticket closes with W2, not W |
| §5.9's admission checks uniformity but not that the physical work before the verdict is population-independent: a `matching` word scan enumerates words before readability and can abort at the word ceiling; an expiring table charges expired rows before the rule | GPT 1 | Accepted: a never-predicting pass is admitted over a plain prefix scan only — no `matching`, no merge, no table with an expiry — refused at `check` otherwise; paired regressions for a hidden word expansion and an expired population |
| A mutation's non-uniform page read without a loop can still abort at the meter (the bypass the first pass asked a regression for) | GPT 1 | Ruling 17 (c): built as the conservative rule pending the author |
| An immutable column in a patch on an unreadable target is a match/mismatch search (`sent` / `NOT_FOUND`) | GPT 6, Grok 2 | Ruling 17 (a): built as the conservative rule pending the author |
| An author condition on an unreadable target is the same search | GPT open question 2 | Ruling 17 (b): built as the conservative rule pending the author |
| The twenty-two regressions exist but none compares the complete observation; two names over-claim; `wa2_retained…` pairs two colliding worlds | GPT 6, Grok 2 | Accepted: one observation helper for the paired tests (the first body, the receipt, the retry, every meter field, committed rows and maintains, effects, stream and snapshot, both device stores); the two tests renamed to what they prove; `wa2_retained…` gains a non-colliding world; the device regression stays in the device crate and §15's list says so |
| Retained unique columns of an admitted hidden row are searchable through a collision (`sent` / `NOT_FOUND`); caps, `after insert`, budget aborts near a ceiling; timing and the logical miss class | both | Residuals, as §15 recorded; the meter restore above removes the logical-cost half |

**2026-09-14 — the third pass, on lane W2 at `b80ad0cb6`** (both lanes
rebased onto main; artifacts
[gpt](./reviews/3000.007-write-authority-implementation-2.gpt.md) and
[grok](./reviews/3000.007-write-authority-implementation-2.grok.md)).
Verdicts: GPT **NOT READY**; Grok **READY WITH CHANGES**. Both find every
row of the table above built as written and the three rulings behind
named guards; what remains is narrower and deeper. Lane W3 builds it.

| Finding | Family | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A uniformly decided page over a hidden prefix charges an examined row before its verdict, so a meter already at the ceiling aborts for one hidden row and commits for none; a uniform rule whose `exists` probe crosses an expiring table charges that table's expired siblings the same way | GPT 1 | Accepted: the verdict of a uniformly decided rule is established from the pinned prefix before any candidate is examined, and a false verdict returns the empty complete page at no charge; a rule probe costs one unit whatever expired siblings it passes, or such rules are excluded from "uniform" for mutation pages; the plain-prefix restriction of row 6 applies to every page a mutation reads, predicting or not, looped or bound (Grok's untested residual) |
| Target resolution evaluates the read rule and allocates the merged image before it can say `NOT_FOUND`, so an `E_BOUND` inside that work escapes the meter restore and tells hidden-refused from absent near a ceiling | GPT 2 | Accepted: the static worst case of resolution is reserved against the meter before the lookup; if it does not fit, both worlds refuse `E_BOUND` before the lookup; if it fits, resolution cannot abort |
| A maintained sum's overflow prints the computed value, from which a hidden contribution can be subtracted | GPT 3 | Accepted: overflow refusals name the maintain and the column, never a value, on the live path too |
| An explicit asset id in a patch to an unreadable target is preserved when it equals the retained placement and validated otherwise, an equality probe on the hidden asset | GPT 4 | Accepted: omitted columns keep their placement; an explicitly supplied id on an unreadable target is validated as the writer's own upload whatever it equals |
| A hand-serialized merge without `merge_site`, bound without a loop, escapes the structural merge prohibition | GPT 5 | Accepted: derived from expression structure |
| Rulings 17 (a) and (b) as built run on the hit arm only: an `upsert` whose patch names an immutable column, or that carries `if`, is `NOT_FOUND` on a hidden target and inserts on an absent one — a new existence bit | Grok 1, Grok 2 | Accepted. 17 (b): the binder refuses `upsert … if` (the card documents the condition on `update` alone). 17 (a): the conservative form changes — on an unreadable target the immutable columns of a patch are ignored rather than compared, on `update` and on `upsert`, so a hidden admitted hit and an absent insert are both `sent`; a readable mismatch keeps its named refusal. The lead's recommendation to the author moves from refusal to this form, because refusal forbade the ordinary profile upsert or reopened the bit; ruling 17 (a) stays open |
| The matching `update … if` on a hidden target is asserted by a single call, not through the observation helper; several other cases still assert less than the complete observation (the readable clash's index name, the failed-receipt case, the revocation tests, the serialized-condition tests) | Grok 3, GPT 6 | Accepted: through the helper, with an intervening revocation step where the case is revocation |
| Receipts written before this repair keep their historical `why` text | GPT open question | No published release wrote lane W's receipt shape; `from_receipt` re-sanitizes a historical body defensively anyway |
| Caps, `after insert`, admitted-write budget outcomes, retained-unique match, timing | both | Residuals, as §15 and the table above record |

**2026-09-14 — the fourth pass, narrow, on lane W3 at `e3187c87f`**
(the three lanes rebased onto main; artifacts
[gpt](./reviews/3000.007-write-authority-implementation-3.gpt.md) and
[grok](./reviews/3000.007-write-authority-implementation-3.grok.md)).
Verdicts: Grok **READY** — every third-pass row built as written, nothing
the repairs touched opens a bit, two low notes; GPT **NOT READY** on
three corners narrower than the last, each with a concrete repair. Lane
W4 builds them; the implementation then lands on the strength of four
passes (ruling 15 asked for two), with a final GPT-only check against the
landed commit and any finding fixed forward, so the rerun of §10.3 is not
held on a fifth round.

| Finding | Family | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Under `read <- not exists secrets[id = 'sentinel']` the pinned-prefix verdict is true on an empty table and false on a hidden-populated one, so at an exhausted budget the empty world charges a candidate and aborts while the hidden world returns empty free: an inverse population bit | GPT 1 | Accepted: a page's attempt charges one examined unit before its verdict in every world; at the ceiling both worlds abort alike |
| `created … [equalities]` takes the metered probe path but the static reservation counts `Created` as zero, so an absent target can abort before insert denial where a hidden one says `NOT_FOUND` | GPT 2 | Accepted: a `Created` with equalities counts as one probe in the worst case |
| Extra equalities on a maintain over an expiring source are tested against the physical, expired-inclusive aggregate row, not the live value | GPT 3 | Accepted, the narrow form: `check` refuses a value equality on a maintain over an expiring source; existence probes on such maintains stay one unit; other equalities are unchanged |
| The historical-receipt regression is single-world; the scalar-result revocation tail bypasses the helper; no immutable-plus-maintain boundary test | GPT 4 | Accepted: through the helper, with the maintain/delivery pair |
| A live-maintain existence probe's inner source walk has no named 0 vs 9,000 pair; a debug print in the exhaustion fixture | Grok (low) | Accepted: the pair is added; the print removed |
| Timing; caps, `after insert`, admitted-write budget outcomes, retained-unique match | both | Residuals, as recorded |

**2026-09-14 — built by lane W4.** Aborting page passes charge one examined
unit before the verdict and charge its probes on both outcomes; capped
queries retain their cut contract.
`created` equalities enter the static reservation. `check` refuses a value
equality on a maintain over an expiring source, with the program-read
rewrite; its existence probes remain one unit. Paired regressions cover
probe ceilings, 0/9,000 expired sources, historical/scalar revocation and
ignored immutables with maintains and delivery on both device stores.

**2026-09-14 — the fifth check, GPT only, against the landed commit
`82034fde5`** ([artifact](./reviews/3000.007-write-authority-implementation-4.gpt.md)).
Every fourth-pass repair built and covered; one channel that W4's own
repair introduced: a false verdict is charged the probes it actually
executed, and `and`/`or` short-circuit on hidden answers, so under
`read <- exists secrets[owner = viewer] and exists permits[owner = viewer]`
an empty world charges one probe and a hidden-populated world two — at the
ceiling, `sent` against `E_BOUND`, with both verdicts false. Disposition:
the uniform page's verdict charges a state-independent, schema-derived
probe allowance (the rule's static probe count) whatever branch decides,
keeping the common attempt charge and the verdict-before-candidate order;
paired regression through the helper for `and` and `or`, empty/hidden and
equality match/mismatch, ample budget and one remaining probe, both
stores. Lane W5 builds it as a fix-forward on main; the implementation
stays landed. Ruling 17 (a)'s form remains the author's.

## 18. The 0.2.9 calibration's findings, and the catch-up landing (2026-09-14)

The Astra calibration on `snapback4@0.2.9`
(`benchmarks/runs/2026-09-14-snapback4-clique-astra-0.2.9/`; seven of seven
green in 6,890 s; holistic 85) scored behind the September 5 Convex and
Supabase cells on three categories: setup_and_docs 78 (Convex 83, Supabase
85), data_modeling 88 (Supabase 94), queries_and_live 74 (Convex 87). The
same-family scorer reads the builder's own narrative and charges the backend
for defects, missing capabilities and documentation that misled, never for
agent or environment loops; its notes name what it charged. `processDevice()`
is named in the README without a signature and the bare call threw from
inside the package. Retained history disappeared under a horizon when a
membership was removed, and the builder synced every conversation unbounded
to keep it. The raw server search over every conversation returned nothing
complete at any scale checkpoint (N_NEVER_COMPLETE), so the builder wrote
that a third-party client cannot trust the endpoint. Index prefixes, bounds
and residency cost learning; Trending needed an explicit ranking model; a
defaulted new column refused adoption while `check` stayed green. Everything
else in the run was the agent's or the environment's. Five lanes build the
answer (CU1–CU5); each decision below is the anchor for the code that
implements it. N=1, same-family, cross-run: a gap of a few points is inside
the noise; the search gap is not.

### 18.1 Search across the viewer's groups is a merge of matching lanes

A `merge` over a words index is admitted when the merge reads `matching q`:
each lane is one scope prefix of the index, read as the matching scan already
reads a scoped search (the first term's words as ordered lanes, bounded by the
meter for a uniform scope, at most 32 for an admitted non-uniform scope),
and the lanes merge into one page ordered by the index's order columns. Each
lane's rule is decided by its prefix, so a lane is one verdict and its page
is complete; the merged page is complete when every lane's is, and a merge
past 50 lanes carries N_WIDE_MERGE as a feed does. The spelling, on the
search card:

```quarry
query everywhere(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
  return merge messages matching q after c last 50 by byRoomWords:
    [m.roomId] for m in members[viewer] first 50 by byPrincipalRoom
```

The same code runs on the device over the held rows, where the partition
holds exactly what the viewer may read. N_NEVER_COMPLETE's rewrite for an
unscoped words scan under a per-scope rule names this form before "read it
on the device". Not admitted: a merge over a words index without `matching`;
relevance ranking (NOT-DOING stands). The binder owns the exact grammar
position of `matching` in a merge; if the parser needs it elsewhere than
above, the card and this section move together.

Built as written (lane CU1): the spelling above is the parser's. A
matching merge lowers to scoped matching scans in the existing lane IR, so
no corpus shape changed; a lane's rule is judged once against its scope
before its words are discovered, so a denied lane returns nothing and
costs nothing that depends on its rows; a rule that is not uniform over the
scope keeps per-row checks and the never-complete note.

**2026-09-15 — W7d, the 0.2.22 H5 envelope.** The search card's
"Search every chat I'm in" section now paves 100–1,000 memberships:
an unscoped words index over full device holdings, or a server walk with
separate membership-batch and matching-result cursors. A result cursor
does not advance membership discovery, and no cursor recovers an unfinished
matching lane. The card records measured examined counts, whole-walk
coverage, horizon and admitted non-uniform scopes' 32-word limits, and the
copy arithmetic that rules
out a per-reader search inbox for this recipe. N_WIDE_MERGE points to that
section. The device witness exposed a continuation bug: an eager generated
word lane tried to validate the merge token as a direct cursor bound to
one word. It now validates the shared search descriptor, as lazy word lanes
already do; ordinary direct-page prefix/generation checks are unchanged.

### 18.2 Retained history survives revocation under a horizon

**2026-09-18 — ruling 36, lane W13k: selection at serve time.** A join whose
membership interval is open when served and is not a continuous renewal acquires
the group's current readable images at the time the stream serves the join.
Membership intervals remain join-time. This replaces "a join acquires the images
that existed at its own commit" and retires "even if a later commit ended the
membership": a join whose interval is already closed acquires nothing, for private
and public tables alike. Retained history keeps what was served; nothing was
served by such a join. A same-commit grant and revocation conveys no authority.
A continuous renewal expands no rows; an expired predecessor's scope precedes
its replacement acquisition.

Selection walks the current group in the serving transaction at its fixed `now`,
descending on the horizon index, or on the audience index without a horizon,
skipping expired rows and checking each row's read rule under the joining
principal's server context (`Context::new(principal, …).at(now)`, `device = false`).
It stops at N readable rows without looking past the Nth, or at the range's end;
there is no probe budget or early stop. A horizonless acquisition delivers every
current readable row. The checked image is the delivered image, at the join's
position. A row written after the join and before serving may also appear as its
later log put; ordinary and staged device application replace the same id, a held
row is not floor-filtered, and a later deletion still deletes.

For a horizoned acquisition, the end marker's floor is the full horizon key of
the Nth delivered row, including its id tie-breaker, or `whole` when the range ends
with fewer than N readable rows, including zero. The cold snapshot uses the same
walk and the device principal's read check; `live_aggregate` keeps the system
runner. At a stable readable state and clock the snapshot and stream floors agree.
Snapshot pages remain separate transactions: intervening moves may change the
selected images, and catch-up repairs them without moving the snapshot floor.
The wire shape `acquisitions[{at, table, index, group, floor?}]`, retained
re-acquisition's unknown boundary until a replacement snapshot, continuation
validation, and snapshot marker charging are unchanged. Join expansion remains
outside the raw-log page limit. `join_completion`, the historical unselected-row
walk, delivered-set tracking, `group_at`, `written_after`, and their row-log index
are deleted. The historical candidate set is intentionally lost: with N = 1, a
newer readable row inserted after the join can replace the join-time candidate;
a read below the acquired boundary is incomplete and falls back as before.

`retain delivered-history` keeps what a device was given whatever the
table's horizon; the horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a join) and
nothing else. A membership removal delivers the scope tombstone and the
device leaves the acquired rows in place; a local read of the left group
answers from them and reports completeness by the same rule it used before
the leave. A snapshot or catch-up that replaces the device's rows keeps the
retained rows of groups the viewer no longer holds. The sync card says so in
one sentence; the device regression runs on both stores. The 0.2.9
evidence: `evidence/m3-retention-probe7.txt` (25 rows `complete: false`
before removal, 0 rows `complete: true` after) against
`m3-retention-unbounded.txt` (26 and 26); the ticket is
`issues/20260914-snapback4-retained-history-with-horizon-drops-rows-on-removal.md`.

Built (lanes CU2 and CU2b). Two losses, not one. The one the builders
saw was the client: a horizon makes the device's answer unknown or
incomplete, and `local.ts` then substituted the server's answer, which for
a revoked viewer is empty and complete (the real client, before: 0 rows
`complete: true`; after: 25 rows `complete: false`, wasm and process
devices). The second was the replacement snapshot, whose `clear_rows`
dropped what it did not re-acquire. The device now keeps a held/left map
per delivered-history table: a scope tombstone marks a group left, the
first snapshot page names the viewer's current audience groups for those
tables (audience prefixes only), and a stream page names the groups joined
in it. A read that pins a left group answers from the device, and the
client never falls back to the server for it, for reads and watches. A
replacement snapshot keeps confirmed rows only for groups the viewer no
longer holds, so a deletion the device missed while away stays deleted. A
read that does not pin a group keeps its old behaviour.

**2026-09-16 — proposed composition, not a contract change.**
[LLP 3000.001](./3000.001-durable-replica-and-write-lifecycle.research.md#proposed-composition-of-retained-and-authoritative-reads)
now plans per-value provenance and a first slice composing independent
retained/current result components. The mixed-query ticket remains open;
the current whole-query fallback, `held` behavior and separate-query recipe
remain implemented. The proposal names the coverage, freshness, authority
and lifecycle decisions required before changing that behavior.

**2026-09-17 — the proposal rewritten and commissioned; still not a contract
change.** The plan in LLP 3000.001 no longer tracks per-value provenance or
adds a server sidecar: the binder proves a returned record's independent
components, the device reports coverage per component, and the client answers
each component from the host that would have answered it as its own query.
This section changes when that implementation lands, not before.

**2026-09-17 — lane MQ implementation.** A binder-proven, cursor-free query that is not declared `held` and returns a record of independent fields, each holding at most one read, with no reading `require`, can compose retained and authoritative reads. A found point read of a delivered left row counts as retained. Composition is entered only when at least one field routes to the server and at least one is retained. Every field that would stay local as its own query comes from a second local observation, and only the server-routed fields come from the original query's one server reply. The result's `complete` is the conjunction of the server word and every locally kept field's word; it reports `fresh: false`, no `since`, and no continuation. Composition is never entered while a predictable write is unobserved; the whole local answer stands until it settles. Offline or on request failure, the whole local answer stands. The server and the wire are unchanged.

**2026-09-14 — lane X1, backend store replacement.** Acquisition and the
held/left map belong to a database incarnation, not merely its schema
generation. `_meta.store_id` is a random 128-bit identity written once on
writable store open, preserved across restarts; sync replies add `store_id`
and requests accept its optional echo; change notifications add the same ID. This is additive within the package
version: requests may omit it and older replies may omit it; clients send
it only after learning it. A mismatch drops confirmed and predicted rows,
staged pages and held/left groups atomically, resets acquisition and the
watermark, reloads the backend even when its generation number matches, and
restarts the snapshot and catch-up. A first identity on a
legacy acquired device requires that same reacquisition; without an identity,
a backwards watermark is the reset signal. The stream reports its actual
head when the supplied watermark is ahead. No old partition becomes fresh
or complete during reacquisition; ordinary online query fallback may still
answer from the current server. Long-poll replies compare
store identity too, so equal-watermark replacements converge on a live client
without extra sync requests on an unchanged database.

The durable outbox keeps unsent and unknown-receipt writes, with the same
operation and row IDs and no predicted images. Known sent or acknowledged
receipts retire their entries without replay to the replacement; an unknown
receipt still retries under the existing per-store at-most-once rule. The
device's existing send revision also invalidates responses captured before
replacement, and the client serializes receipt settlement with sync. These
are internal transport facts; the public read/write card is unchanged.

**2026-09-14 — X1 review round 1.** The reset transaction also persists
`device:backend-required`, naming the replacement store (or `legacy` for
an identity-free regression). Acquisition and device reads refuse until
successful backend installation clears that obligation in the installation
transaction. Retry, an offline reopen, and a process restart retain it;
same-number generations do not excuse adoption. Installation checks its
store/send capture atomically, so a delayed adoption cannot discharge a
newer store's obligation. Each native owner also checks the persisted backend
digest before acquisition or reads: another owner's successful adoption does
not make an old in-memory interpreter current at the same generation.

A client incarnation revision advances immediately when sync or a change
poll observes replacement, before waiting for the network queue. Freshness,
remembered server answers and watch snapshots are invalidated at observation.
Every asynchronous read is fenced through return, caching and notification;
an answer from the earlier revision is retried. New reruns use the current
server until the replacement partition has completed acquisition, so the
observation cannot relabel old local data as belonging to the new revision. Sync and change replies are
fenced too. Legacy regression inference compares a reply to that request's
watermark and checks that its captured persisted watermark and send revision
are still current. A delayed page behind another owner's advancement is a
stale response, never permission to delete retained history.

Orphan adoption compares the persisted source and destination identities.
Foreign known receipts retire without a send; unknown and unsent entries
transfer with their IDs and no predictions. If a known receipt's ownership
cannot be resolved, it remains in the orphan store with its identity; unknown
entries may transfer, and a later observation or reopen retries the deferred
receipt. Same-store known receipts keep their acknowledgement. An orphan
whose source identity was never recorded keeps unresolved known receipts;
it cannot safely assert ownership in an identified replacement.

First identification of a legacy acquired device deliberately loses its
held/left history even when the backend is actually the same database. That
history has no persisted store provenance, and a group already left cannot
be reacquired. The regression records **50 retained rows before, 0 after**
on both stores. Same-store snapshots with an already known identity retain
the ordinary history policy. Release activation, rollback, program adoption
and backwards client clocks do not themselves change the store identity.
The Node IndexedDB tests model abort before commit and loss of the response
after commit across rows, metadata, stages, bases and outbox; they do not
establish real-browser termination or physical-power-loss durability.

**2026-09-14 — X1 review round 2.** `GET /changes` accepts the optional
`store_id` echo and answers immediately when it differs, even at equal
head and with the production 25- or 20-second wait. It also answers a head
below `since` immediately, returning the actual lower sequence. `/probe`
adds `store_id` and `seq`; reconnect checks them before local freshness or
the long wait resumes. A matching identity resumes without an extra sync.
Disconnect synchronously downgrades cached watch freshness. Round 1 already
clears and notifies watch snapshots in the reset turn; that finding did not
reproduce. The live process-device and wasm regressions cover replacement,
and both clients already discard a foreign `after` sent during acquisition.

A matching identity with a lower head means the same store was restored or
shrank. It durably refuses `E_STORE_RESTORE`, stops the client's sync retry
loop, and refuses device reads and new sends. Neither freshness nor
completeness is claimed for the old partition. Its watermark and outbox
remain in place; retry, offline reopen, backend adoption and recovery of the
