### 20.22 Total reads: the first query a builder writes is the one that works

The eleventh Astra-medium calibration, on 0.2.27, scored holistic 89. Across the
last four runs the scorer's recurring deductions in `queries_and_live`,
`data_modeling` and `scale_and_limits` are one family — N_WIDE_MERGE and "the
1,000-chat server-search ceiling", "incomplete meter cuts", "explicit
completeness obligations", "a two-cursor server walk", "substantial serving
structures", "feed caps remain explicit" — which the program read as *the builder
must reason about bounds before they can write a query*. A proposal followed: let
the engine plan the batched walk, make completeness something the engine
computes, and stop adding surface for a wave.

The family is right and the mechanism was not. The forms were probed against
the **published 0.2.27 binary** rather than the corpus (the finding, with the
schemas, scripts and raw counters of every probe, is
`benchmarks/runs/2026-09-17-snapback4-bounds-probes/` — `FINDING.md`, `README.md`,
`RESULTS.md`):

- **No bound was hit at runtime in any of the last four lanes.** Their evidence
  directories hold no `"complete": false` answer, no `E_BOUND`, no cap refusal;
  every degradation ratio passed; every bounds-related correction loop took
  seconds to minutes and was self-attributed. What was scored is anticipatory:
  notes read, cards read, designs changed in advance, limits disclosed. Three
  friction reviews had already said so ("narrative, not measurement"), and each
  wave answered with more guidance.
- **The query a builder writes first already works more often than `check`
  admits.** `merge messages matching q … [m.roomId] for m in members[viewer]
  first 1000` is complete with `next` at 1,000 chats when hits are sparse (1,115
  examined). It is cut, with no `next`, only when every chat holds hits — and
  only because `rules::lazy_merge` excludes a lane whose prefix holds a
  `WordPrefix`, so matching lanes read a page each (24 examined a chat) where
  plain lanes read one head (the same 1,000 lanes as a plain merge: 3,040
  examined, complete). The Clique scale fixture *is* that dense case: all 99,090
  messages contain "coffee", and the hub principal is in all 1,000 conversations
  before the 10% checkpoint.
- **One false incompleteness.** A `where` predicate's inner read runs at the
  outer page depth, so an inner top-1 with more rows sets `page_overflow` and a
  fully walked answer reports `complete: false, cut: null`. On a device that
  costs a server round trip as well, because the local client falls back on any
  `complete === false` rather than on `unknown`.
- **Merge-on-read Following is complete with one cursor and is never cut at 500
  follows; whether it passes the 3× gate depends on the measurement path.** A
  500-lane page costs about 2.5 ms more than a 5-lane page (1,540 examined
  against 115). Behind 2 ms of fixed request overhead that reads 2.05–2.32× (the
  first probes) and 2.5–3.0× p50, 2.7–3.1× p95 (the bounds bar's runner);
  through two fresh builders' own bare clients, where a small page costs 0.3 ms,
  it reads 9.0× and 7.3×. The finding first said "passes"; the bar's "before"
  corrected it. With the public tables synced the merge `runs: device`, and
  `follow` and `post` predict, which the installed recipe's `follow` never does;
  `check`'s rewrite to the recipe is nonetheless defensible at this width until
  the per-lane head cost falls.
- **`check` speculates and `dev` is silent.** N_WIDE_MERGE is a static
  `lanes > 50`; a read that *is* meter-cut under `dev` prints nothing. LLP
  2000.000 §6 promised the reverse on both counts.
- `queries_and_live` averaged 88.8 over 0.2.16–0.2.22 and 82.2 from 0.2.23 on;
  the break is the wave that paved global search with the two-cursor walk. N = 1
  a run: suggestive, no more.

The invariant this section adopts: **every read the language admits either
completes or returns a continuation the SDK follows without the author; the
author never writes a number the schema states; `check` notes only certain
consequences, and costs are measured facts in `dev` and `test`.** No ceiling is
raised, no planner is introduced (LLP 3000.009 §4.5 and §13 stand: every change
below is an access strategy or a lowering, in interpreter charge order), and
every relaxation is confined to the uniform class.

#### 20.22.1 Without rulings: correctness and cost (wave 13a)

Order inside the wave: item 1 and §20.22.5's merge work (the unique merge
position, the outcome-time `next`, the drain-cut continuation) are one engine
lane and go first, because that lane closes a P1 that loses rows silently on the
published binary; item 2 rewrites the same `merge_lazy` and builds on it rather
than beside it.

1. **A predicate's inner read does not overflow the page.** `where` predicates
   evaluate at `page_depth + 1`. Regressions are the `grouped-defect/` probe's
   v1 and v2, on the server and both device stores. *Built by lane W13a, landed
   `40900b0ff`.*
2. **Matching lanes are lazy.** For the uniform class, (scope × discovered word)
   lanes join the existing head heap; the per-scope verdict before word
   discovery keeps its meaning; the 32-word cut applies to admitted non-uniform
   scopes, while uniform expansion is meter-bounded (§20.24.1). Non-uniform merges keep the
   eager traversal byte for byte. Then measure index-covered heads (no row fetch
   until a lane wins; never for a table with expiry). Acceptance: the search
   card's dense 1,000-membership fixture answers its first page complete with
   `next`, on the server and both device stores; a Clique-shaped fixture (every
   row a hit) likewise. *Built by lane W13f, landed `04f8ba1c39`: 340 / 1,540 /
   3,040 examined at 100 / 500 / 1,000 dense memberships, complete with `next`,
   were 2,400 / 8,358 (cut) / 8,880 (cut); index-covered heads measured as a
   store-interface change (about seven files) and not built, so the Following
   ratio question stays open.*
3. **The device falls back on `unknown`.** A meter cut, a word cut and a
   cursorless top-N are answers the server would repeat. *Built by lane W13b,
   landed `7cca16da2e`; the same rule routes a composed component (main's
   retained-read composition), and a component that waited on presence is
   unknown, not merely incomplete.*
4. **A measured cut is printed.** `dev` prints the operation, persona, meter,
   site and numbers when a read is cut (loopback only, as `Read.meter` is);
   `snapback4 test` flags a read past half a ceiling on the fixture. *Built by lane
   W13b, landed `7cca16da2e`.* *Open for the
   author:* N_WIDE_MERGE leaves the default notes — its arithmetic moves into
   `check --cost` ("≤ N lanes × (1 head + 1 lookahead) + N discovery of 8,192").
   (An earlier draft added "and the Following variant stops naming the recipe for
   a merge that fits"; the bar's "before" withdrew that — see the bullet above.)

#### 20.22.2 The meter bounds a query's lane source and prefix loop (ruling 23)

*Built by lane W13c (with §20.22.3, §20.22.4, rulings 28–29 and the lane-source
seek fix), landed 2026-09-17; its blind review — round 1 GPT NOT READY (one High:
a matching `exists` still took the word merge's lookahead) / Grok READY, the fix
round, then a recheck READY / READY WITH CHANGES (one Low, applied) — is under
`./reviews/3000.007-w13c-implementation.*`.*

In a **query**, a merge `for` lane's domain and a `for` loop's domain may be a
prefix read with no `first N`:

```quarry
query everywhere(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
  return merge messages matching q after c last 20 by byRoomWords:
    [m.roomId] for m in members[viewer]

query unreadTotal():
  n = 0
  for m in members[viewer]:
    n = n + m.unread
  return n
```

The scan runs in ascending index order until the prefix ends or the examined
meter cuts it; a cut is `complete: false, cut: 'meter'` as any cut read is.
`check --cost` prints "lanes ≤ meter" and "rows ≤ meter", as `range` over int
expressions prints "steps ≤ meter" (§5.7): a prefix has an intended bound — its
group — and the meter holds it. Conditions: the read rule is uniformly decided
by the pinned prefix (a non-uniform prefix is refused with the `first N` rewrite
and N_NEVER_COMPLETE's index advice, because unbounded it would run to the
ceiling on every call); queries only, including a `fn` inlined into one —
mutations and jobs are unchanged, since a cut there is an abort for that user,
not a cap; a **returned** page still needs `first N` or §20.22.3's cap, because
output must stay bounded. No schema cap is required. That matters: the 0.2.26
builder added `cap 1000 by principal` to `members` only to justify `first 1000`,
and from M3 every `check` reported the price — `chat.addMember`'s insert arm
never predicts — which no narrative file mentions. A bound invented to satisfy
the parser can cost a capability.

A loop the meter cuts has no continuation; a total over a group that may outgrow
one read is a `maintain`, and the card says so. A lane source the meter cuts is a
dead end until §20.22.5. Serialized shapes already in the corpus do not change;
a new bound variant, if the lane chooses one over a lowering, is additive, and
the device and server ship together as for `Scan.filter`. The diagnostic for a
boundless prefix in a position that does not admit one names `first N` (today it
is E_QUARRY_TYPE "wrong number of keys" with a rewrite that asks for a full key).

#### 20.22.3 A capped group needs no restated bound (ruling 24)

Where the pinned prefix carries a schema `cap` (`Schema::group_bound`), a prefix
read with no `first N` is the whole group, lowered to `first <cap>`:

```quarry
table members:
  roomId: rooms
  principal: principal
  unread: int
  unique byRoomPrincipal: roomId, principal
  cap 100 by roomId
  read <- exists members[.roomId, viewer]
  update <- exists members[.roomId, viewer]

query roster(roomId: rooms):
  return members[roomId]

mutation send(roomId: rooms, body: text <=4000):
  insert messages { roomId, body, at: now }
  for m in members[roomId]:
    update members[m] { unread: m.unread + 1 }
  return null
```

It applies to a returned or bound page, a shape's nested page, a mutation's page
and loop (where it supplies the complete-capped-group proof §5.7 requires) and a
job's inner loop. The cap is changed in one place. `first <cap>` stays legal with
no note and no forced rewrite; `first N` keeps meaning "a page of N", and an
explicit bound below the cap in a predicting loop keeps today's refusal. Refused
at `check`, with the rewrite: a boundless read in a returned or mutation position
whose prefix has no cap ("declare `cap N by cols`, or page with `first N` and a
cursor"); a returned whole group whose cap exceeds the examined ceiling (the form
means *the whole group*, and one read cannot examine it — page it). In a query
loop an uncapped or oversize group falls under §20.22.2. The rule "the literal
page bound must cover the schema cap", its diagnostic text, and the cap clause of
N_NO_CURSOR's rewrite go; E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT's rewrites name the boundless form.
An index is inferred as it already is for a page read (one index leading with
the pinned key; otherwise `by index`, today's refusal).

#### 20.22.4 `exists` over a prefix or a matching read (ruling 25)

```quarry
query grouped(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
  return members[viewer] after c first 20 by byPrincipalRoom
    where exists (messages[.roomId] matching q)
    { roomId, hits: messages[.roomId] matching q last 3 by byRoomWords }

query hasMessages(roomId: rooms):
  return exists messages[roomId]
```

In a program expression, `exists t[keys]` whose keys are a strict prefix of one
index, optionally with `matching q` or `by index`, is a top-1 scan evaluated
below the page. Inside a `where`, a modifier after an unparenthesised inner read
belongs to the *outer* read (§5.6's rule: "a non-index modifier ends `where`"),
so the inner read is parenthesised there, as `by` already must be; the
unparenthesised form is E_QUARRY_MODIFIER_SCOPE with the mechanical rewrite that
adds the parentheses. (The preview the author ruled on showed the `where` form
without them; the ruling is the capability, and the parser's existing scoping
rule decides the spelling.)

The scan never sets `page_overflow` and never owns a cursor. Verdict, meter,
expiry and horizon are a `first 1` page's; a cut before a row is found answers
false on a capped query, as a filter read does today. Keys that complete a total
unique key keep today's one-probe meaning. In a mutation it is a page like any
other: a uniform, plain, non-expiring prefix only (§17). **Rule probes are
unchanged.** Not included: `exists … where`. One point for the lane to establish
and the review to check: on a device, a *positive* answer from held rows is exact
whatever the horizon, and only a negative answer past a horizon is unknown.
Today's refusal of `exists messages[roomId]` — "no unique index over 1 column …
rewrite: `unique byColumn: <column>`" — tells the author to make the room's
messages unique by room; it goes with this change.

With §20.22.1 (1–2) this makes "search every chat I'm in, grouped by chat" — the
Clique brief's literal requirement — one query with one cursor: 1 request at
1,000 chats when five hold hits, 3 when fifty do, 51 of 180 examined when all do
(9,000 examined to show every chat with its newest three hits; the two-cursor
walk spends 792,359 enumerating all 24,000 — different outputs, and the grouped
one is what the screen draws), and a meter cut carries `next` because it is a
filtered page (§5.6).

#### 20.22.5 A cut merge: what ruling 26's design review found (two defects shipped; the drain cut continues; windows shelved)

`merge_can_continue()` withholds `next` from any merge the meter or the word
limit cut, because the cursor has no per-lane positions; and a lane source is
scanned below the page, so it can never own `next`. Those two facts are the whole
reason for the two-cursor walk, whose card text — "do not exchange them",
"change both `first 50` bounds together", "never advance past a cut" — is the
author executing a composite cursor by hand. The 0.2.27 builder documented that
protocol correctly and then wrote a walker that never follows the result cursor.

Ruling 26 commissioned a design and a two-family blind review before anything is
built, with five fixed points: the uniform class only; one opaque cursor and no
new `Read` field; ordered windows past one read's budget, each page `complete:
false, cut: 'meter'` until the last; `usePage` discharges a followed cut; the
two-cursor card section stays until the continuation lands. Four rounds followed,
two families each; the design that survived them is
[LLP 3000.007.000](./3000.007.000-merge-position-and-drain-cut-continuation.spec.md),
whose §9 links all eight reviews (`./reviews/3000.007-cut-merge-continuation-design*`).
Three things came of it.

**1. Two defects of the shipped engine, found by both families and reproduced by
execution on the published 0.2.27 binary** (tickets under `issues/`; probes with
the bounds probes):

- *A merge cursor bypasses `E_CURSOR_INDEX`, and a merged position is not unique.*
  Merge lanes are bound with `cursor: None` and the shared cursor is attached
  afterwards (`bind/program.rs`), so a merge never reaches the check a direct page
  gets. Over `by byRoomAt: roomId, at` — non-unique, no `id`, refused for a direct
  page — the merge compiles clean and skips a row inside one lane (2 of 3
  returned); over `unique byRoomAt: roomId, at` it skips the second of two lanes'
  rows at one `at` (3 of 4). Every page says `complete: true`. P1. The fix the
  design prefers: every stored index key already ends with the row id, so the
  merged position becomes `(suffix values, row id)` — compared, carried in the
  token and sought on — and no author changes a schema.
- *A merge's `next` survives a non-uniform read that runs later in the query.*
  §20.3's round-1 rule ("the non-uniform class withholds `next`") holds only when
  the non-uniform read runs first, because eligibility is read when the page is
  recorded. No cross-world leak was shown. P3. Fixed by deciding a merge owner's
  continuation when the outcome is built.

**2. A cut while *draining* continues (Part D).** A lazy merge opens one head a
lane and then drains a heap. A cut while opening leaves unknown heads and no safe
position. A cut while draining — every head known — leaves the emitted rows a
true prefix of the merged order, provided the loop stops at the first head it
cannot establish, *after every pop, emitted or duplicate*. Today the refill is
skipped once the meter is cut and the loop is not, and the token is gated on a
`has_more` a short page never sets. D stops the loop; the owning traversal
records a continuation candidate from its own facts, and the outcome publishes it
for a merge owner unless the whole answer is the server's non-uniform class (a
direct page keeps §5.6); the token is today's v1. A drain-cut page is what any
cut read is — `complete: false, cut: 'meter'` — now with `next`. D's scope is
the merge whose lanes open within one read and whose drain is expensive; a
single-scope `matching` page is such a merge. **Not delivered: the fourth fixed
point.** The hook's discharge of a followed cut needs `cut` to mean "the
continuation covers everything this answer is missing", and three review rounds
each found a contribution that invariant missed — a starved shape read, a live
aggregate emitted partially computed, a read stopped by an inherited cap with no
failed charge of its own, a nested page's word cut under a direct owner. It was
descoped at the three-round limit: `usePage` is unchanged, and a followed cut page
still holds the aggregate incomplete, as a cut filtered page has since wave 3.
*Status: built by lane W13a and landed 2026-09-17* (its blind implementation review: READY WITH CHANGES from both families — one defect, the stop-after-pop applied after an opening cut, fixed in the review round; reviews under `./reviews/3000.007-w13a-implementation.{gpt,grok}.md`). Round 4 read revision 4 READY WITH CHANGES from both
families with nothing Critical or High; the changes were specification wording,
fixture arithmetic and landing inventory, and revision 5 — LLP 3000.007.000, the
design of record — applies all of them. Two points the families disagreed on were settled on the code
rather than by vote: a filtered merge of eight lanes or fewer cannot drain-cut at
all (its opening batch asks for `take + 1`, so a lane's buffer outlasts the page —
the positive that claimed otherwise was withdrawn, and the existing
`feed_forms.rs` assertion that such a capped page has no `next` stays as the
tripwire), and "the traversal's own word cut" is the runner-wide `word_cut_at` at
record time, exactly what `merge_can_continue()` reads today, not a new per-site
fact. The implementation gets the wave's ordinary two-family review of the
integrated diff; it goes first in wave 13 because it carries the P1.

**3. Windows (Part O) are returned to the author, recommended shelved.** Two
rounds did not converge: a run of expired lane-source rows has no position a live
row witnesses (the families disagree on whether an expired key may be named); a
lane-source row that moves behind the walk's frontier takes its lane with it, and
no stateless digest can see that; a word boundary in a fixed matching scope has no
representation; counting only witnessed word lanes toward 32 moves NOT-DOING by
the back door. And a probe run after round 2 shows the mechanism is not needed.
The **grouped** one-cursor search — `members[viewer] after c first 20 … where
exists (messages[.roomId] matching q) { roomId, hits: … last 3 }` — is a filtered
page, whose cut has carried `next` since wave 3 (§5.6): at 9,000 memberships with
hits deep in the order it is meter-cut at 8,194 examined, returns `next`, and
finishes on the second request. Between them the two one-cursor forms cover the
space: the merge for one globally ordered first screen, within one read (about
2,700 dense chats once matching lanes are lazy; Clique's envelope is 1,000); the
grouped page for everything, grouped by chat — which is what the Clique brief
asks — at any number of chats. What windows would add, a *global* order wider
than one read, no reference application asks for, and the windowed design could
not deliver it either.

**Ruled, 2026-09-17 (ruling 26, amended):** Part O is shelved, and the fifth
fixed point is re-ruled — the two-cursor section, `init --recipe search`,
`src/search.ts` and `searchChats` are replaced by the grouped one-cursor form in
the landing that carries §20.22.1 item 1 and ruling 25, rather than waiting for a
continuation that is not coming. If a reference application ever needs a globally
ordered merge wider than one read, reopen it from these reviews; the honest
starting point is a snapshot contract, not a cleverer token.

#### 20.22.6 Ruling 21, re-scoped (ruling 27)

§20.7 was ruled yes on 2026-09-14 and never built (the feeds lane met the
refusal and used the viewer's own read audience). A second probe changed what it
is for: with `sync public` on `posts` and `follows` — what every recent builder
ships, recipe or not — the six-line merge already `runs: device` and `follow`
and `post` predict. Subset sync is therefore an **acquisition** lever (every
device holds the whole public corpus: 48,200 posts at the Clique envelope), not
a prerequisite for Following on both hosts. It stays ruled yes and is decided on
a number nobody has yet: per-table cold rows and bytes for the reference app
(the 0.2.26 review's H5). If built it needs the partition-inversion blind review
(LLP 3000 decision 4) and an answer for an author's own rows — an author is not
in their own follower audience, and a second inferred audience is NOT-DOING's
"union audiences".

#### 20.22.7 What follows, and what does not

After §20.22.1–4 land: the search card's "Search every chat I'm in" becomes two
one-cursor queries and a paragraph saying which to use when; `init --recipe
search`, `src/search.ts` and `searchChats` go with it (ruling 26 as amended,
§20.22.5; lane W13e). *Open for the author:* whether `held` and E_QUERY_HELD go
too, decided on the wasm device's measured cost for ~3,000 examined a page.
Whether the feeds recipe stops being the default rewrite for a wide Following
merge is **not** decided here: it waits for keys-only heads (§20.22.1 item 2),
measured on a low-overhead client at 5 and 500 follows — about a fourfold cut in
per-lane head cost is what a 3× ratio needs there. The wave carries a surface budget —
card lines, codes a reference app emits, and numeric literals a reference app's
author had to choose (the feeds recipe: about 23; merge-on-read: a follow cap, a
horizon, a page size) — all of which must fall.

Measured with a **bounds bar** in the ceremony bar's mould, before and after
(`snapback4/loopbar/bounds.mjs`, `BOUNDS.md`; landed `5d2fb848a`): N ≥ 3 fresh
builders, a hub-scale seed (a member of 1,000 chats whose every message matches,
500 follows, a 900-follower author), four tasks (global search grouped by chat,
Following, a roster with an unread total, Trending); wall time, loops by
attribution, lines of Quarry, literals chosen, notes fired, cards read, the
runner's own `complete`/`cut`/`next`/meter observations — bounds *met*, against
bounds narrated — and the count of bound vocabulary in the builder's final
report, which is the scorer's input. Eleven N = 1 calibrations have moved
`queries_and_live` ±8 between adjacent runs with no query-surface change.
**The "before" on 0.2.27 is recorded** —
`benchmarks/runs/2026-09-17-snapback4-boundsbar-before/`, four valid green runs
(9.6–10.5 minutes at load 11–76) and two attempts the host invalidated. What it
read: every builder lost the continuation by returning `group(page, …)` first;
half met the lane-source seek defect (P2, fixed in W13c); three of four chose
the card's two-cursor walk, at a hundredfold cost in requests against the
fourth's grouped page; every one wrote `N_WIDE_MERGE` into its report as a
limit it had not met; not one verified page was cut. It also corrected §20.22's
Following claim (the bullet above).

**The horizon P1 (§21.8 L14) is closed — lane W13g, reviewed at `e4f274b479` and landed on main 2026-09-17.** A device page over a
horizoned table is complete by its **acquisition boundary**, carried on the snapshot wire as an
additive field `acquisitions[{at, table, index, group, floor?}]`, not by a count. The device
could not infer the boundary from row images (the stream serves current images; a mutable key
can move below the floor before a device sees it; two histories with different floors produce
byte-identical payloads), so the server names it. Five blind rounds shaped the field, every one
split GPT NOT READY / Grok READY until the last read READY from both: a retained re-acquisition
stays `unknown` until a full replacement snapshot (what the server no longer holds is not "the
group at N"); a keyed completion names only a delivered, current, readable selected row; the
snapshot continuation is validated (`after` echoed; `E_GENERATION` on a skipped page or a
premature catch-up); markers are charged to the page budget (5,000 empty groups paginate in
158 pages); boundaries load once per transaction. The last three rounds were all one question —
*when may a completion be published at all* — and each hole was in the lead's own specification
of the proof, not in the design: the join-time selection floor leaked a deleted row's key by its
presence; "same key = unchanged" leaked an unselected row's pre-join key; "any log event for the
row" counted another principal's membership events, which `_log` keys by the content table and
the group's canonical JSON. The rule that stands: a completion at K is published only when every
current readable row of the group at or above K was delivered by this acquisition or has a
post-join **row** transition (`put`/`del`) in the log — a function of the delivered rows, the
current readable images and the post-join row-event set, each of which the device receives.
Costs recorded, not gated: +18 KB metadata at 1,000 groups; wasm +10.8 KB gzip; one indexed
existence probe per candidate blocker on the rare path. *Open for the author:* selecting the
acquisition at the **current** state over **readable** rows only would make the boundary the
Nth delivered key by construction and close the family at its root — including two pre-existing
properties of the join-time selection, that it counts hidden rows in its N slots (the marker's
form can reveal one) and delivers fewer rows when a selected row is deleted before delivery — at
the cost of §18.2's "a join acquires the images that existed at its own commit". **Verified by execution by the derived-data session on main `c2823d4954`** (`40adcfb3e0`: §21.10, §21.8 L25, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-17-snapback4-derived-probes/` "After the repair"): the horizon-gap probes page as the server does, and LLP 3000.007.001's gate 2 passes — a device holds 0 rows beyond its horizon where 0.2.28 held 1,165 (58.3%).

**The starved-preview P2 that W13e's walk disclosed is closed — lane W13h, reviewed at `e8d3a8b858` and
landed on main 2026-09-17.** A cut page never shows a row it could not finish. The returned shape runs in a
loop *after* the source page is materialised (the ticket's first draft said `scan_prepared`; W13e's review
corrected it), so a row whose shape the meter starved is dropped with every later row and `next` positions
before the first unfinished rule-visible row — exclusively after a finished predecessor, else inclusively at
that row with a v3 token (exclusive v1/v2 bytes unchanged; §5.6's refinement; LLP 3000.007.000 §3 item 4's
`emitted ≥ 1` counts fully computed emissions; an inherited cap that stops an eager range/list lane is a read
cut). The 9,000-membership grouped walk returns no chat without its hits: request 1 empty with `next`, request
2 all three with previews (examined 9,015 → 13,112: the dropped row's discovery is paid twice; a cut inside the
predicate keeps §5.6's position). Six blind rounds. The first four rechecks each found a program in which
a *static ownership analysis* — deciding whether the returned value is the shaped page or carries its raw source
— rewound a returned raw page or lost its owner: a used auxiliary shape, a conditional value, a loop-carried
binding, a record joined with a non-page, a collector that embeds the raw page; the fourth also found the
analysis could fail to terminate. **The analysis was descoped**: the rule applies to a loop the binder marks as
the query's returned shape expression (`return <read> { … }`, the read inline or a single-definition variable used
nowhere else), where the rewind is safe by construction; every other program keeps the pre-W13h behaviour (the
starved row stays with its empty shape; `next` after it) — the card's grouped form is the marked form. Cost:
wasm 467,476 gzip at the head against 505,030 at the base. *Open for the author (tickets filed):* a returned shape
that cannot finish under any fresh meter mints an inclusive `next` at its own row, and a follow makes no progress
— a runtime refusal on the no-progress follow (recommended), a `check --cost` note, or both; and whether the
general form deserves a *runtime* ownership rule (the runner knows what it returned) rather than a static one.

One more dead end, met by the bar's own reference-app author and reproduced
(`group-drops-next/`): a query that pages `after c` and returns
`group(page, 'roomId')` can never publish `next` — every full page answers
`complete: false, cut: null` — and `check` says nothing. The engine is honest
(§19.2: a page whose position is not returned is incomplete); the author is not
warned. `group` returns every element of its argument, so carrying the page
through it would be sound. *Open for the author:* carry the page through
row-preserving helpers (`group`, `sort`), or note it at `check`.

Not addressed here: a rolling-window ranking (§20.21); a mutation page over an
expiring table (§17's restriction — whether it relaxes for a uniformly decided
prefix is a review question); job sweeps, where builders' designs were genuinely
uneven and the period-ranged chunk wants paving on the jobs card. Instrument
note: the 0.2.27 "failed search midpoint" is a flat curve (p95 8.229 / 7.940 /
7.316 ms) missing a downward line by 0.12 ms; the midpoint rule needs a
tolerance band.

**2026-09-18 — the open items above are ruled** (§14, rulings 34–38, on the
lead's recommendation): the page's continuation is carried through
row-preserving helpers (34, lane W13i); the no-progress follow becomes a runtime
refusal (35, lane W13j); the horizon acquisition is selected at the current state
over readable rows (36, design amendment then lane W13k); the runtime ownership
rule, `held` and the feeds demotion wait for the bar's "after" (37, 38). The
wave's pin is 0.2.29; the bar's "after" runs on it with the instrument
byte-identical to the "before".

**2026-09-18 — the bar's "after" on 0.2.29 is recorded**
(`benchmarks/runs/2026-09-18-snapback4-boundsbar-after/`): three green runs of
three, 7.8–10.7 minutes at load 52–70, the instrument byte-identical to the
"before", the pin unchanged after every run; the pin itself was fetched back
from the registry and re-probed by the derived-data session (`08292107c4`: the
horizon repair and ruling 32 are in it). Against the "before": the
`group(page, …)` dead end 4/4 → 0/3 — every builder wrote the card's grouped
one-cursor form (two verbatim), which needs no `group`, so ruling 34 (W13i)
closes a hole the bar no longer walks; the two-cursor walk 3/4 → 0/3 (50 or 51
requests for the 99,090 hits against 5,005); Snapback-attributed loops 2/4 →
0/3 (the `$value1` defect, W13c); `check` notes fired 4/4 → 0/3
(`N_WIDE_MERGE`, `N_SPARSE_FILTER`; the one note in three runs was
`N_MAINTAIN_DELTA`, a true disclosure); `N_WIDE_MERGE` narrated as an unmet
limit 4/4 → 0/3, and bound vocabulary in the reports 31–43 → 15–29 a run;
loops 3–5 → 0–3, all transport probes. Not one page of any verified walk was
cut, before or after. What did not move: literals 35–55 → 34–44; the runner's
Following ratio (2.6× / 1.7× / 2.5× at p50, the same merge-on-read shape —
ruling 38's keys-only measurement is still owed); and the surface budget went
the other way — the README 1,657 → 1,977 lines, the cards +249 lines in all
(only the search card fell, 320 → 242), and the builders read more of them
(1,380–2,494 → 1,872–4,797 lines requested). Two builders of three wrote the
meter-bounded lane source's sentence ("can exceed the opening meter at larger
fanout") into their reports as a limit they had not met: the note was retired
and its sentence took its place. Rulings 37 and 38 stay deferred as ruled: no
non-canonical returned shape appeared (every builder wrote the marked form), so
no ownership rule is commissioned; the calibration on 0.2.29 decides whether
wave 14 removes surface.

**Ruling 35 is landed — lane W13j, reviewed at `1e5fda7f68` and landed on main 2026-09-18.** A request that begins at an
inclusive v3 cursor at position P, returns an empty page and would mint an inclusive `next` at the same P refuses with
`E_SHAPE_EXCEEDS_METER` — "the returned shape at *site* examines more than the *N*-row meter on its own; bound it
(`first N`/`last N`) or move it out of the returned shape" — on the server and both device stores, as a structural
refusal the client sees as `denied` with the code, like `E_QUERY_HELD`; the card section says what to do. The first
request (the one that drops the starved row and mints the inclusive cursor) is unchanged, a follow whose row finishes
is unchanged, an exclusive-cursor follow never refuses, and an empty page for any other reason (a filter rejecting
everything, a range end) is not a refusal. The pinned residual test is the positive on all three hosts. Two blind rounds (GPT / Grok): round 1 READY WITH CHANGES / READY — the refusal named the first site that spent the meter rather than the blocked shape; round 2 READY / READY once inherited exhaustion was distinguished ("the returned shape at <shape> cannot finish: the meter was spent at <site> before it ran").
The `check --cost` note for a returned shape whose own bound exceeds the ceiling still waits for the cost arithmetic.
Cost: wasm 410,412 gzip at the head.

**Ruling 34 is landed — lane W13i, reviewed at `c8962a937c` and landed on main 2026-09-18.** A query that returns
`group(page, …)` or `sort(page, …)` — directly, through a variable bound once, or one level nested — publishes the
page's `next` exactly as if it returned the page, with the page's own `complete`/`cut` class, on the server and both
device stores; a helper that drops or reorders elements (`first`, `last`, `at`, `slice`, `min`, `max`, `concat`)
keeps today's behaviour, and `group` over anything but a page carries nothing. The returned-shape mark (§5.6) is
untouched: a `group` over a marked returned shape neither gains trimming nor loses it. The `group-drops-next/` probe's
query now walks to completion with `next` on every full page. Two blind rounds (GPT / Grok): round 1 READY WITH CHANGES / READY — the diagnostics' returned-page sites omitted the pages a helper carries; round 2 READY / READY once `returned_page_sites` unioned the runtime's cursor-bearing helper origins into the retained cursorless walk (a cursorless grouped page still emits no `N_NO_CURSOR`; no note changed its trigger). Cost: wasm 410,459 gzip at the head.

**Ruling 36 is landed — lane W13k, reviewed at `31c953e610` and landed on main 2026-09-18, after its design (rev3, two blind
rounds READY WITH CHANGES from both families).** The horizon acquisition is selected at the current state over readable
rows: an acquiring join walks the group's current rows descending in the serving transaction at its `now`, skipping
expired rows, evaluating the joining principal's read rule under that principal's server context, and stops at N
readable rows (no lookahead) or the range's end — every current readable row for a horizonless table; the end
marker's floor is the Nth delivered row's full horizon key, or `whole` when fewer than N readable rows exist
(including none). A join whose interval is closed when served acquires nothing, private and public tables alike —
§18.2's "a join acquires the images that existed at its own commit" is amended to the current readable images at the
time the stream serves the join, and "even if a later commit ended the membership" is retired; non-acquiring joins
expand nothing; the cold snapshot walks the same way under the device principal's context (`live_aggregate` keeps the
system runner), so snapshot and stream floors agree at a stable state. The boundary is therefore the Nth delivered key
by construction, and W13g's completion proof — `join_completion`, `written_after`, `group_at`, the delivered-set
tracking and the `_log_row_seq_kind` index (dropped on open) — is deleted with no remaining caller. The two
pre-existing properties of the join-time selection (hidden rows counted in the N slots; fewer rows delivered when a
selected row is deleted before delivery) go with it; the historical candidate set is intentionally lost (with N = 1,
a newer readable row inserted after the join can replace the join-time candidate). The wire shape, retained
re-acquisition's unknown boundary, continuation validation and marker charging are unchanged. Three blind rounds (GPT / Grok): round 1 NOT READY / READY — a renewal before delivery erased the acquisition and the predecessor's scope, and the cross-host witnesses stopped at the server; round 2 NOT READY / READY — joins sharing a commit were confused with one another; round 3 READY / READY once spans and transitions were keyed by `(seq, ord)` with one acquisition per live chain at its first join. Cost: wasm
410,459 gzip at the head.

**2026-09-18 — calibration #13 on 0.2.29 is recorded, and the question above is answered by the numbers**
(`benchmarks/runs/2026-09-18-snapback4-clique-astra-0.2.29/`; the packet is #12's, frozen, only the pins changed).
Holistic **89**: setup_and_docs 92, data_modeling 85, authorization 96, queries_and_live **78**, writes 93,
offline_sync 97, media 96, background_work 94, client_integration 90, diagnostics 94, scale_and_limits 83, operations
89. The grader passed 101 of 119 assertions (the 9 fails are absent iOS-simulator artifacts for M6 and M8 — Hermes
exports only — and the 9 not-run are M7, by the packet's design) and filed one major defect: broad-prefix search truncated at the 32
first-term-words expansion bound (45 `kx*` words → 20 hits, `complete:false, cut:"words"`, no `next`) — the same bound
every bounds-bar builder narrated as a limit it had not met, now met by a grader at a realistic corpus. Fitted over the
eleven runs on the comparable instrument (0.2.16 → 0.2.29): overall [90, 91, 88, 91, 88, 88, 91, 90, 89, 85, 89], mean
89.09, sd 1.81, slope −0.23/run, drift −2.27 — still beyond one standard deviation, softened from −0.30 by this run;
queries_and_live −1.04/run (78 is the series low); setup_and_docs 92 with a drift of −3.55 (> its sd); scale_and_limits
back to 83 (its −11.18 drift is #12's 65, the load-shaped reading); data_modeling −0.39/run; offline_sync +0.30/run (97,
the series high). **89 is the series mean, not a move above it.** Ruling 40 (§14, ruled the same day) named the test in
advance: "a recovery toward 90 reads as the decline having been noise, and a third consecutive fall at or below 85
earns the removal wave." 89 sits in that condition's gap — neither branch fires, as ruling 40's revisit (§14) records;
the removal wave is not earned by the ruling's own terms, and the binary this paragraph's opening posed is retired
with it. What the fitted line still says is recorded without being over-read: the drift is beyond one
standard deviation on a series whose sd is under two points, and the category that fell to its low did so on one bound
the tool names without advising — `cut:"words"` and no `next`, the same limit every bounds-bar builder wrote down. Under
ruling 40's method the next questions are, for each correction this builder paid, what the tool told it: the five
Quarry-language corrections were each answered by a diagnostic that carried the rewrite (the builder says so), and the
search bound was not. The lead's recommendation to the author: wave 14 takes the prefix search's expansion bound as its
one capability item (a continuation past 32 words, or a refusal that says which words), and tests removal against one
named candidate with a measurement — the bar's surface-budget row (+320 README lines, +249 card lines) is the evidence
ruling 40 says advice quality does not answer. No wave is commissioned by this record.

**2026-09-18 — calibration #14 on 0.2.30 is recorded, scored against its pre-registered prediction**
(`benchmarks/runs/2026-09-18-snapback4-clique-astra-0.2.30/`; the prediction, `52c74a223c`, filed during the builder
phase and resolved with the record). Holistic **90**: setup_and_docs 88, data_modeling 82, authorization 96,
queries_and_live 85, writes 96, offline_sync 96, media 98, background_work 87, client_integration 92, diagnostics 95,
scale_and_limits 82, operations 88; the grader 101 of 119 (the same nine native-artifact fails and nine M7 not-runs as
#13); one major defect, the application's; the builder's two hours paid **no Snapback-attributed correction**. The
prediction's strong criterion is met in full: the four shapes §20.23's repairs targeted are absent from the builder's
evidence — no `int has no fields` cluster (20 occurrences in #12), no `bool ?`/`!= null` repair (6), no ESM restart loop
(7), `N_NO_HORIZON` not in the log — and no Snapback-attributed loop replaced them; `diagnostics_dev_loop` 95 sits above
its eleven-run fitted line (94.4). Its category criterion did not partition the space: `setup_and_docs` 88 sits on its
line (88.4; 0.4 is 0.14 of the category's sd) — neither branch fired, as the file's author acknowledges, and #15's
prediction gets a threshold per category with an explicit null band. Two claims, kept apart: the mechanism result is
positive (20 → 0, 7 → 0, 6 → 0 on the targeted shapes) and the magnitude result is null. The pre-registration's author,
holding to the conclusion the file pre-committed to (recorded in their name): "right about repair order, wrong about
magnitude — advice quality is worth doing because it is correct, a rewrite that recommends a silent bug is a defect
whatever the score does, but on this evidence it is not the lever holding the score down, and the surface-removal case
is correspondingly stronger than §20.23 argued." The builder's zero Snapback-attributed loops is its own attribution — a
floor on Snapback's share, not a count of it. What this builder paid instead, each
with a rewrite: `upsert cannot spell id` ×8, `require … else CODE` ×6, `len is not part of Snapback 4` ×4, and the same
`likeCounts[.postId] is not a rule term` #13's builder met. Fitted over the twelve comparable runs: overall mean 89.18,
sd 1.83, slope −0.18/run, drift −1.82 — the holistic decline no longer clears one standard deviation; `setup_and_docs`
(−0.54/run, drift −5.4) and `data_modeling` (−5.3) still do; `queries_and_live` 85 is seven points above its line on a pin
that changed nothing in search (this grader did not probe the 32-word bound; its d-03 names the grouped form's
conversation-id ordering and missing per-chat continuation beside it; the bound's absence from this run is not
evidence it stopped mattering). The decline has localized — to `setup_and_docs` and to `data_modeling`, the one category
no wave has gone at directly. Under ruling 40's refined condition the lead's recommendation to the author, commissioning
nothing: wave 14 keeps the search expansion bound as its one capability item, names its removal candidate and the
category it predicts to move — the README's first screen and the cards a cold builder reads, predicted to move
`setup_and_docs` — and goes at `data_modeling` directly, each with a pre-registered threshold and null band, for
calibration #15 on the pin that carries it.

### 20.23 A horizon's index must order the group by mint time, and the advice is the only place that can enforce it

The twelfth Astra-medium calibration, on 0.2.28, scored holistic 85 with
`scale_and_limits` at 65 on a 54,982 ms cold first feed. The measurement that
followed (`0f4ff08e98`) refuted the acquisition-volume hypothesis but surfaced
one table: `follows`, declared `sync public` with no horizon, ~1.7 MB of a
6.7 MB cold acquisition for one persona.

**The detection was not the problem.** That builder declared 17 `sync` lines,
12 of them unhorizoned. `check` emitted exactly **one** `N_NO_HORIZON`, and it
named `follows`, on every check they ran. The suppression rules — `maintain_for`,
`needs_whole_partition`, a `cap` over the sync group, a one-row group — are
doing their job precisely.

**The advice was.** For `sync public` the group prefix is empty, so
`columns().starts_with(&prefix)` held vacuously for every index and the
`BTreeMap` returned whichever name sorted first. The builder was told:

```
rewrite: sync public last 500 by byPair
```

`byPair` is `follower, target`, a unique pair index with no time order. That
horizon delivers the 500 alphabetically-highest pairs — an arbitrary slice of
the follow graph. The dependence on name order is directly falsifiable: renaming
`byTargetTime` to `aaTime`, changing nothing else, changes the recommendation.

**Why the advice string is the only place this can be fixed.** Applying the
note's own rewrite compiles to `check: ok — no problems` and the note
disappears. The semantically correct horizon produces the identical verdict.
Static compilation cannot distinguish "the 500 most recent follows" from "500
arbitrary follows", because both are well-formed; only a measurement against
real data would ever show it, and nothing prompts one once the note is quiet.
There is no downstream gate. A builder who follows the advice is paid a green
check for making the app worse, which is a stronger reason to ignore diagnostics
than any amount of noise.

**The rule.** An index qualifies as a horizon only if the column immediately
after the group prefix is minted in time order: a `time` column, or `id`. Ids
qualify by construction — 48 bits of milliseconds then entropy, Crockford
base32, so they sort as text in the order they were minted. Admitting `id` is
not a convenience: it is what keeps `sync to members[.conversationId] last 500
by byConversation` over `by byConversation: conversationId, id` correct, which
is the paved horizon in the corpus. When no index qualifies, the advice is to
add the ordered index first — the branch that already existed and was, before
this, unreachable for any table with any index at all.

Two smaller corrections travel with it. A `cap` whose `by` is not the sync group
is now named in the note, saying what it does not bound: the 0.2.28 builder
wrote `cap 1000 by follower` on a `sync public` table, so they had already tried
to bound it, and an unqualified note reads as not having noticed. And the note
now names `check --residency`, which already reports acquired bytes per table
per persona — the ranking the note itself cannot carry, and which no builder in
the programme has been observed to run.

**The general claim, which is what the next wave should inherit.** Twelve waves
have improved detection. This defect is not a failure to notice; the notice was
exact. It is a failure to convert a notice into a repair the author will make.
Where a diagnostic's rewrite is the last point at which correctness can be
decided — as it is here, and as it is for any advice a green check will
subsequently silence — the rewrite deserves the scrutiny of a runtime
invariant, not the scrutiny of a string.

### 20.24 Wave 14, commissioned under the author's sign-off (2026-09-19)

On 2026-09-19 the author gave the wave-13 lead a general sign-off ("you have
signoff to do whatever you think you need to do"). Decisions taken under it are
the lead's, recorded here the way §21.8 records the derived-data lead's, and
none of them publishes a package. The inputs are calibration #14's reading
(§20.22.7) and the pre-registration discipline it was scored by.

- **L14.1 — the wave is pre-registered before any design exists**
  (`issues/20260919-pre-registered-predictions-for-calibration-15.md`): one
  threshold per category with an explicit null band, every number from the
  twelve-run fit extrapolated to run 13 — `setup_and_docs` ≥ 91 confirms / ≤ 85
  refutes, `data_modeling` ≥ 86 / ≤ 80, `queries_and_live` ≥ 84 / ≤ 75 — with a
  mechanism criterion beside each score, scored from the evidence files by string
  search first. A lever absent from #15's pin makes its prediction void, never
  failed or confirmed. The holistic number is not a criterion.
- **L14.2 — W14a, the prefix search's expansion bound, design first.** A
  design lane weighs a continuation past 32 words, a refusal that names the
  words, and the meter replacing the fixed number (ruling 23's form), under
  LLP 3000 §7's disclosure rule and §20.22.5's cut vocabulary; a two-family
  blind review; then a build lane with its own review. The evidence is #13's
  one major defect, every bounds-bar builder's narration, and #14's d-03.
- **L14.3 — W14b, removal tested against one named candidate with a
  measurement: the README's orientation volume.** Ruling 40 admits exactly
  this; the scorers' `setup_and_docs` deduction across #12–#14 is orientation
  volume and never documentation error
  (`issues/20260918-setup-and-docs-deducts-for-volume-not-for-error.md`); the
  bar's "after" measured the README at 1,977 lines and the cards +249. The
  candidate: the README as the orientation of its first screen with a reference
  map, everything else moved — not deleted — into cards read on demand, with a
  documentation surface budget enforced like the wasm ratchet. A measurement and
  design lane first (what cold builders read, section by section), a blind
  review, then an edit lane.
- **L14.4 — W14c, `data_modeling`, no new lane.** The lever is rulings 39 and
  41 — MC1's maintained columns and W15c's value-index tie-break — because the
  scorers' recurring deduction is the explicit ranked table, its hour-leading
  indexes and the validation probes a builder writes around them; the
  prediction is pre-registered and rolls forward unchanged if the pin lacks them.
- **What this does not do:** publish (0.2.30 was the author's; the next pin is
  the author's), change a ceiling, or commission anything the pre-registration
  cannot score. Wave 14 lands lever by lever; calibration #15 runs on the pin
  that carries them, with cal14's frozen packet and only the pins changed.

#### 20.24.1 W14a as built: a uniform scope's word expansion is bounded by the meter (L14.2)

**Landed — lane W14A-BUILD (the spike, round 0 and round 1 squashed), reviewed at `bcbdf0c122` and landed on main 2026-09-20.** Recheck round 5 at bcbdf0c122: READY from both families — GPT with no findings and no objection to the ruling, Grok with one Low (two leftover 'nothing else is copied' sentences) applied at landing. Web device +9,526 B gzip (451,094 against main's 441,568); the ratchet rises to 452,000 with the attribution in `metrics/wasm-budget.json`.

A uniformly admitted matching scope discovers every first-term word that fits the
query-wide meter and merges those words as ordered lanes. There is no separate
32-word limit for that scope. A full eligible page can continue in suffix order;
a terminal full page need not carry a cursor. A denied scope performs no word
discovery. A discovery or opening read-meter cut leaves an incomplete page with
no continuation. Only an owning uniform lazy traversal with established heads,
a fully shaped emitted position and final-answer eligibility can continue a
resumable read-meter cut during its drain. Heap exhaustion clears the whole page
to `data: null` with no `next`, even during the drain; step exhaustion aborts and
output exhaustion can refuse after materialisation. Following a cut does not
clear `usePage`'s accumulated incompleteness. Returned-shape trimming, device
horizon coverage and the live-read qualification still apply.

An admitted non-uniform scope keeps at most 32 first-term words; hitting that
bound records private `word_cut_at`, without stopping later lanes. On the server
its public `cut` is null; on the device, and on uniform-class reads that carry a
word cut, it is `words` (a simultaneous meter cut takes precedence). The existing
non-uniform full-page class remains never-complete with no `next`; below the word
bound its public `cut` is null. Incompleteness alone never instructs the builder
to narrow the term. The control remains 33 discovery entries, 32 examined rows,
server `cut: null`, and no continuation.

The allocation ledger is the doc-comment of
`snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/word_heap.rs` and the W14A-BUILD-R1
journal entry. Its three classes are retained interpreter storage, bounded
store scratch, and shared images. Allowances precede the growth they cover:
field/scope vectors, normalization and tokenization, discovery keys and words,
lane descriptors and heads, every fetched row/key including discarded entries,
optional fields, dedup nodes/splits, page positions and cursor decode/re-encode.
Compiled scan/expression trees and schema images are borrowed; generated lanes
share their scope's remaining terms. A cursor is validated once per distinct
input, while arbitrary bound expressions still evaluate per lane. Empty
remaining terms do not allocate a candidate tokenizer. The 1,000-word/eight
4 KiB dead-literal fixture walks all 50 pages without copying those 32,768 IR
bytes per lane. Container capacity replacements are prepaid, including the
first allocation. A consumed Vec-to-VecDeque transfer allocates nothing.

A finite schema-max-row multiplier is impossible for unbounded nominal IDs and
principals. The narrow store-interface exception uses borrowed stored bytes:
charge the actual serialized-image bound, then decode/copy it. SQLite reserves
`2 * page_size` once per read before its first byte-length preflight, streams
each row's `octet_length(r.data) + length(i.k)` in the scan's order with the same
bounds and limit, and charges `2 * length + 64` before stepping to the next row.
On refusal the statement resets without stepping again. Within one preflight
execution the first row's resident copy (bounded by one page-size buffer per
column, two registers) precedes its charge; every later row's copy follows the
previous row's charge. Across executions the reserve prepays the first
execution's first copy once per read and later executions' first copies are
charged immediately after, bounded by the same constant. No other priced payload
is copied before its allowance. Resident TEXT/BLOB payloads are copied; overflow payloads
are never read by the preflight. Register growth allocates the entire new size,
and reset frees the register buffers: the reserve alone bounds neither a batch's
cumulative allocation nor repeated executions. The page size is read once from
the connection when the store opens. The subsequent payload statement uses the
actual-length allowances already granted, without charging them again, and Rust
never first owns its JSON string; memory stores share row images and prepay copied
keys/results. The optional PostgreSQL adapter preflights aggregate byte lengths
inside the existing transaction before its owning fetch. These are cumulative
allocation allowances, not measured live RSS and not raised ceilings. Existing
general expression evaluation and rule-probe accounting remain their own
contracts. The native B-tree allocation probe measured at most 1,408 bytes on
one of 4,096 inserts; the documented node/split bound allows 5,488 at insert
4,095, with ID payload separately prepaid. The lead rules (2026-09-20, under the
author's sign-off) that these allocation and allowance-ordering statements price
the interpreter's allocations and the store's payload copies: row images, keys,
and registers holding resident payloads, with the ordering exception above.
The storage engine's per-execution statement storage (`VdbeCursor`/`BtCursor`,
column-offset arrays from `allocateCursor`, sorters and non-payload registers)
and its page cache are shared images outside the ledger, never interpreter heap.
Statement execution storage is bounded per execution and transient, freed by the
same reset chain; it is measured and pinned, not charged. With lookaside disabled
and warmed statements, `sqlite_empty_scan_statement_storage_is_pinned_and_transient`
in `crates/snapback4-core/tests/word_heap.rs` measures 1,952 native bytes for one
valid empty scan's two executions (`limit = 1`), pinned at 2,048 B (rounded up to
the next 1,024 B). Its 1,000-scan counterexample measures 1,952 B per scan,
1,952,000 B cumulatively, 976 B peak live and zero live bytes after every reset
pair, under a pinned peak bound of `2 * 2,048 B`; the meter charges 8,192 B once
plus 105 B per scan, 113,192 B total. These are requested native capacities,
including the probe's size headers, not allocator slack or RSS; warmed page-cache
images are outside that execution measurement. This exclusion is the same for
every store scan in the engine: no read on main has charged execution storage,
and charging it only on the word path would make its cost incomparable with
every other read's. This is a scope ruling; no charge changes.

The disclosure evidence comprises three pairs on server, both device stores
and wasm. Denied-scope populations of 0 and 9,000 have byte-identical production
replies, including cursors and expiry. The expired-word pair has two sub-pairs:
with ample headroom, an unswept expired-only word leaves full replies identical;
at the 46-entry discovery boundary, no expired word completes discovery in 46
seeks, while the extra expired word attempts entry 47 and returns the existing
empty meter-cut page with no `next`. That is the existing time-hidden existence
bit of metered expired work under LLP 3000 §§6–7, not a new channel. Devices
acquire while live and advance the query clock without sweeping or resyncing;
wasm spends the preceding index budget in the program because its harness has
no ceiling override. The admitted non-uniform pair admits Alice to r1/r2,
keeps her visible coffee row in r2, and adds 33 Bob-owned matching words in r1.
Membership-and-author server rules give equal full envelopes: the visible row,
incomplete, null cut, no next. Hand-inserted online-only device/wasm candidates
compare data/completeness/continuation, deliberately excluding cut (`words` in
the added-population world); legitimate snapshots exclude those candidates and
produce identical empty full envelopes. None claims equality at arbitrary
populations or budgets.

The grouped cut fixture examines 8,193 rows on its empty cursor-bearing first
page and 906 on its second, returning three fully shaped groups: 9,099 total.
The old attempted 8,194th read disappeared through descriptor admission after
an inherited cap. A companion with the matching membership last on the cut
page retries that row inclusively and returns all three groups (8,193 + 815 =
9,008). An interrupted existence predicate retains that inclusive retry. If a
retry interrupts the same membership at the same input position with nothing
emitted, continuation is withheld: `complete: false`, `cut: 'meter'`, no `next`.
This applies to filtered pages without a returned shape too; no cursor can loop
forever at that membership. The final-answer continuation veto is unchanged.
Ordinary range/list lanes retain their previous descriptor allowance; exact
accounting for them is a later named lane. Uniform direct matching is always
lazy because the merge position appends the row id when its suffix lacks it;
admitted non-uniform matching keeps the eager traversal.
C2d still loses completeness to its cursorless auxiliary `last 1`, even
when its uniform owning search gains a continuation. The non-uniform twin
asserts private word-cut evidence as well as the absent continuation.

First-page counters on the rebased tree (heap is the charged allowance in bytes,
not a measured bound). All cells are complete with null cut; pages at 31 words
and above return 20 rows and `next`, and the 0/1 cases return 0/1 rows with no
`next`. Server rule probes are 1; device probes are 0. Rows written are 0.

| Words | Examined | Index entries | Steps | Output B | Server SQLite heap B | Device memory heap B | Device SQLite heap B |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 0 | 1 | 6 | 12 | 15,779 | 6,466 | 14,617 |
| 1 | 1 | 2 | 10 | 109 | 23,703 | 11,513 | 22,541 |
| 31 | 31 | 32 | 120 | 1,991 | 317,857 | 220,505 | 316,695 |
| 32 | 32 | 33 | 123 | 1,991 | 323,881 | 223,640 | 322,719 |
| 33 | 33 | 34 | 126 | 1,991 | 355,505 | 252,375 | 354,343 |
| 45 | 45 | 46 | 162 | 1,991 | 427,793 | 289,995 | 426,631 |
| 200 | 200 | 201 | 627 | 2,031 | 1,520,573 | 932,520 | 1,519,411 |
| 1,000 | 1,000 | 1,001 | 3,027 | 2,031 | 6,995,773 | 4,076,520 | 6,994,611 |

The 45/200/1,000-word walks return all 45/200/1,000 IDs over 3/10/50 pages.
The latency observation used 60 samples after 3 warm-ups per case, at host load
17.71/21.65/29.23 then 19.20/21.40/28.78 (1/5/15-minute averages). These are
in-process engine/device queries in the optimized test profile, not HTTP timings.

| Host | First-page p50 / p95 µs | Whole 45-row walk p50 / p95 µs |
|---|---:|---:|
| Server SQLite | 729.167 / 947.708 | 2102.542 / 2646.708 |
| Device SQLite | 689.416 / 1591.750 | 1784.917 / 8160.416 |
| Device memory | 173.125 / 306.667 | 646.209 / 1085.875 |

No ceiling, Quarry form, serialized value, cursor version, device acquisition
boundary, `outcome_next_cursor` final-answer veto or existing corpus case changes.
`ReadCut` remains `meter` / `words`. `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` remains named-open; d-03's
grouped membership order remains named-open. W14a changes the expansion bound,
not either of those separate product questions.

#### 20.24.2 W14b as built: the README is a 300-line orientation; the reference lives in tested cards under a docs budget (L14.3)

**Landed — lane W14B-EDIT, reviewed at `490ee9cfac` and landed on main 2026-09-20.**
`packages/snapback4/README.md` is the orientation of the first screen and nothing
else: `use identity password`, the profile schema and its operations, session
persist and restore, the platform opener, avatar upload then placement, and
pending/denied/sent handling — in-file, password-only, 300 physical lines (2,004
before). Every reference section moved by heading into the card that owns its
subject: four byte-identical Quarry blocks reused rather than copied, six moved
whole with their tests, three cards new (`app`, `presence`, `shapes`), every
"README §n" pointer in the cards, fixtures, apps and the loopbar brief
retargeted — nothing deleted, every Quarry block still compiled. The cards grew
from 5,621 to 6,332 lines; README + cards fell from 7,625 to 6,632. The docs
budget is `metrics/docs-budget.json` (`readmeLines` 300 — the achieved count
rounded up to the next ten, the wasm ratchet's rounding at this scale), enforced
by `scripts/docs-budget.mjs` from `scripts/test.sh` beside the wasm step: it
fails above the budget and when the README's self-containment test fails, and
